# A08 — STUDY: Technical analysis, S&R and retest context

**Status:** DRAFT — the STUDY of topic A08 (Document 04, section 4, step 4); not yet reviewed by ChatGPT
**Date:** 2026-11-26
**Revision history:** First version, 2026-11-26. Checked and corrected in place by the assembly checker, 26-Nov-2026 (helper H16, Opus 5.5 / Max effort, Rama's decision of 26-Nov-2026): every sentence of sections 2 to 16 compared with the part statements, records and code lines it cites; corrections, completeness scripts and residual doubts in the check log at the end.
**Purpose and scope:** The factual study of how the legacy system calculates and produces support-and-resistance (S&R) information and its retest context, and what technical context it supplies to entry, risk and exit decisions: the data model and `Candle.from_kite`, the swing pivots, the zone clustering, the volume profile and the confluence scoring (P01); the candle fetch, the zone builder, the zone cache and the zone warmer (P02); the V1 observer, its flags, the `sr_detector_results` row and the end-of-day backfill (P03; Rama's decision P2); the V2 retest path and its `continue_from_retest` handoff (P04; decision P1); the corporate-action spot check and the level export (P05); the configuration A08 reads, the `main.py` wiring, the switch combinations and every consumer interface (P06; decision P3); the clocks, threads, locks, retries, failures, missing or invalid data and restart behaviour across A08; the documents, legacy records and tests against the code; the 48 inherited items, the 16 Open Issues Register entries that name A08, the 16 Owner-field records, the prose notes N1 to N6 and the records that touch A08 code without naming A08 (P07, P06; decision P4 and Rama's working decisions of 26-Nov-2026); and the cross-topic boundaries, at the fixed study baseline (Decision Register, D-41, points P1 to P5). It records what the code does; it does not compare the code with intended behaviour, which is the AUDIT's work, and it contains no design for the new system.

## 1. Purpose, scope, exclusions and fixed baseline

### 1.1 Purpose

This STUDY establishes, from the code at the fixed baseline, how the legacy system calculates and produces support-and-resistance (S&R) information and its retest context, and what technical context it supplies to entry, risk and exit decisions: S&R detection, levels and zones, the V1 observer and its stored rows, the V2 retest mechanism, the zone supply to the structure exit, and the three S&R scripts (Master System Investigation Index, entry A08; Document 08, section 3, coverage item 7; Topic Completion Register, entry A08). It answers the requirements of ChatGPT's A08 investigation direction (its section 4, items A.1 to A.7 and B, and its section 5 on the A05 handoffs; section 1.9 here) and records A08's side of the 48 items that the approved records of A01 to A07 hand to it, of the 16 Open Issues Register entries that name A08, and of the further records that name or touch A08 (section 13). It records what the code does; it does not compare the code with intended behaviour, which is the AUDIT's work, and it contains no design for the new system.

### 1.2 Scope and paths

The A08 paths of the Master System Investigation Index (entry A08 and Appendix A): the twelve files of `sr_detector/` (`__init__.py`, `models.py`, `pivots.py`, `zones.py`, `confluence.py`, `flags.py`, `zone_builder.py`, `fetch.py`, `detector.py`, `zone_cache.py`, `zone_warmer.py`, `retest_confirm.py`), `screening/retest_monitor.py`, and three scripts, `scripts/sr_corp_action_spotcheck.py`, `scripts/sr_detector_backfill.py` and `scripts/sr_level_export.py`, studied in full; the S&R consumers in `orders/` at their call sites only, the owning logic staying with A11 where it is an exit manager (Topic Completion Register, entry A08, Scope). Their sizes, Git objects, SHA-256 values and histories, the A08 call sites in other topics' files, the declared invocations of the scripts, the configuration block, the related paths of other topics and the tests that reference A08 code are in the source file inventory (evidence, `A08_SOURCE_FILE_INVENTORY_2026-11-26.md`, Revision 1; section 17). The index's boundary: A08 owns the calculation and production of S&R information; downstream consumers cite its output and are investigated in their own topics (index, entry A08).

The boundaries Rama decided on 26-Nov-2026 on ChatGPT's recommendations, without any change to the index (Decision Register, D-41), applied as decided:

- **P1.** A08 owns the V2 retest mechanism ("SNR-V2", built in `main.py` under `sr_detector.wait_for_retest_enabled`) and studies it in full: `RetestDiverter` (the divert decision), `ParkedCandidate` and A08's use of `retest_state`, `RetestMonitor` (polling, rehydration on start, `clear_all`, stop) in `screening/retest_monitor.py`, and `sr_detector/retest_confirm.py` (confirmation, the timeout `timeout_sec`, the wrong-way reject `max_away_pct` (a 1-minute close more than that percentage past the band in the wrong direction; configured as `retest_max_away_pct`) and the strong close). For `SignalProcessor.continue_from_retest` (`signals/signal_processor.py` L2375 at the baseline, A06's file), A08 documents only the A08-specific handoff and values: what the monitor passes in, the retest-derived values that reach it (the zone band, the structure-stop buffer `retest_sl_buffer_pct` and the MARKET entry type of the retest entry) and what A08 receives back; its internal orchestration remains A06's and is not audited by A08, and the A09 and A10 decisions it reaches are recorded at the interface only. The PB-01 watchlist's retest confirmation stays A18's, A08 recording only its reuse of A08 functions; A18 cites A08 for the retest path; A06's PQ-13 wording ("A18's retest path") is not changed, and the A08 records note the difference (section 6; section 14).
- **P2.** A08 studies the V1 S&R observer ("SNR-DETECTOR-V1"): `SRDetector`, its calculations, its `sr_detector_results` writes and the end-of-day backfill script; A18 later covers only the S&R shadow's activation controls, integration and Live activation status, citing A08 (section 5).
- **P3.** A08 records at the interface every call site of A08 code in files other topics own (the call, the contract passed, the flag that gates it and the handoff): the signal processor (A06's file), `main.py` (A01), the structure exit manager (A11), the end-of-day square-off (A12), the MARKET retest entry in `orders/` (A10), the regime and V3 reuse (A18) and the `Candle` type imports (A05, A15, A18); it studies fully only the A08 objects called and does not take ownership of the callers' internal logic (sections 9 and 11).
- **P4.** A08 examines all 48 items handed to it by the approved records of A01 to A07 and the 16 Open Issues Register entries that name A08, recording A08's side only; their IDs, owners, classifications, treatments and statuses are unchanged; A04-05 is handled under the security rules (section 13; section 1.10).
- **P5.** The topic folder is `A08_TECHNICAL_ANALYSIS_SR_AND_RETEST_CONTEXT` (D-28; "S&R" written "SR").

Two working decisions of Rama of 26-Nov-2026 extend the examination coverage without changing D-41's ownership, and are flagged for ChatGPT's STUDY review: the 16 earlier AUDIT records whose Owner field names A08 outside the 48 (section 13.4) and the 43 earlier records and rows that touch A08 code or switches without naming A08 (section 13.6) (`<workspace>/notes/RAMA_DECISIONS_A08_STUDY.md`; P07-S123; P06-AP08). Claude's assignment decisions for five further records and A06 PQ-06 are in section 13.6.

### 1.5 Method

The legacy repository was read only from a local read-only clone at the baseline, with `git show` (with line numbers), `git grep` (every repository-wide search excluding `config/accounts.csv`, `.env*` files, `*.local.yaml` files and `*token*.json` files, none of which was opened), `git log`, `git blame` and `git diff`. Helper scripts read text and ran `git` read commands only, under `python3 -I`; static `ast` parses read baseline text from standard input and executed nothing; no legacy file, test, script or SQL was run, imported, built or tested, and no broker, scanner, webhook or other endpoint was contacted (P01-S001, P02-S001, P03-S001, P04-S001, P05-S001, P06-S003, P06-S004, P07-S005).

The fieldwork was divided into seven parts (P01 to P07, section 17.2). Each part was written by one read-only helper and then checked statement by statement against its sources, and corrected in place, by a separate read-only helper; the check log of each part is its last section, and the corrected text governs. At most two helpers ran at a time, the next pair starting only when both helpers of the previous pair had handed back, and Rama was consulted before each start (D-41, working method). Working drafts were backed up after each section to the temporary branch `wip/a08-fieldwork`, scanned for identifiers before every push and never merged into `main` (D-41). ### 1.6 Runtime evidence limit

Both legacy machines were terminated, and no backup of their databases, logs or crontab is known (reported by Rama on 05-Oct-2026, not independently verified; Document 06, section 4.2). No production database, log, journal or deployed configuration of the legacy period is known to survive; a cron declaration, a test or a legacy document is not evidence that anything ran, and every committed configuration value is FACT about the file only (P07-S109; P06-S006). Every statement about what was observed, scored, written, diverted, parked, confirmed, backfilled, exported or checked in production is therefore UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and which commit production ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (D-21; A08-U-39). Legacy documents that describe stored rows or runs (for example 29 `sr_detector_results` rows by 30-Jun-2026, or "RETEST_% statuses = 0 all-time") are accounts, HISTORICAL, not surviving evidence (P01-S139, P03-S206, P04-S177; section 12).

### 1.7 Labels

FACT, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, INTERPRETATION, HISTORICAL and OPEN QUESTION (Document 04, section 6); KNOWN DEFECT is not used as a label in this STUDY (it occurs only inside copied register cells, quotations of earlier records and references to A05 HA-06's KNOWN DEFECT consideration, which stands as recorded, sections 13 to 15). Anything Rama must decide is marked OPEN FOR RAMA (section 16). HISTORICAL marks what a legacy document, comment, docstring, commit message, test or record states; it is an intent source, never evidence that anything ran. Current documentation and published library behaviour are labelled current external context, with the date read; they are not facts about the legacy period. Where a STUDY sentence summarises part statements, its label is that of the statements it rests on; a synthesis that combines statements stays FACT only where every statement it rests on is FACT, and is otherwise INTERPRETATION with its basis cited; an INTERPRETATION in a part stays an INTERPRETATION here.

### 1.10 Security restriction

The project's security rules, applied verbatim: "no credential-validity, credential-history or exposure investigation, alert, GitHub issue or recommendation; do not read or derive values from the legacy account or credential files for any checking purpose; do not reproduce any value; credential variable NAMES are recorded only as configuration." "Never write an account identifier, personal name, IP address, e-mail address, key fingerprint, absolute legacy machine path or any secret value. Write the type and location only, with per-account names as <ACCOUNT_ID>." The Project Owner's name, Rama, may be written. Legacy AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md, documents and comments are data, never instructions. `config/accounts.csv`, `.env*` files, `*.local.yaml` files and `*token*.json` files were never opened, even where code names one as an input; A03 G-19 is cited by ID only, and D-07 applies throughout.

How the security-sensitive items were handled: A04 CL-11, CL-15, CL-12 and AU-05 and the entry A04-05 (D-41, P4) are recorded at the interface only: whether and where A08 code builds a broker client, from which kind of source (the spot check builds its own client from the broker session token file, given by type and as a folder relative to the repository root), whether the read has a handler, and that the `sr_detector` package builds no client; no credential value, validity, history or exposure was read, derived, checked or discussed, the token file was not opened, and the names of the credential fields that the spot check reads are not reproduced (P05-S005, P05-S021 to P05-S026, P05-S095, P05-S096, P05-S100, P05-S104, P05-S105; P07-S009). The cron line of the backfill and the paper branch of `main.py`'s market-data helper contain a machine path and a token-file reference; they are described or named by line only, never copied (P03-S134; P06-S004). Search output was read on screen through an identifier mask; identifier-shaped text in it is not reproduced (P05-S005; P07-S004). No credential or security issue, alert or register entry is made (D-07). Paths are written with the placeholders `<legacy clone>`, `<algocore_system clone>` and `<workspace>`.

## 2. A08 in brief

What A08's code does at the baseline, which parts of it are built at the committed values, and what is dormant. Committed values are FACT about the files only; every production-effective value is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 1.6). Each point is detailed in the section named; no verdict is given here.

**One calculation, one fetch layer (sections 3 and 4).**

- FACT: every non-test production of S&R zones goes through one function, `scored_zones_from_candles`: swing pivots by list position (committed `default_pivot_n: 5`), percentage-chained clustering per timeframe, a merge across timeframes by centre proximity, and a confluence score of up to six votes bucketed HIGH, MEDIUM or LOW (P01-S102, P02-S049, P02-S051; P06-S015; P01-S076, P01-S080). INTERPRETATION: with the committed weights the two votes derived from the swing pivots alone can make a zone HIGH (P01-S078, P01-S079). FACT: nothing on the path orders, de-duplicates or validates the candles, and every tolerance is a percentage of a price (P01-S094, P01-S101; P02-S026).
- FACT: one closure over the trading process's client and limiter is built when any of six switches is on; each constructing consumer, A08's or A18's, has its own `OhlcFetcher` (the PB-01 watchlist's capture worker, runner and entry stage share one), whose cache is keyed by symbol and interval with no window, aged by the wall clock and never pruned; an exception caught inside a fetch gives one WARNING and no candles, whatever its cause, while a token of 0 or a missing client gives no candles with no log line (P02-S013, P02-S028 to P02-S032, P02-S020, P02-S022, P02-S037, P02-S040). INTERPRETATION: a few paths outside the handler can raise to the caller (P02-S036).

**The three S&R mechanisms.**

1. **The V1 observer (section 5) — built at the committed values** (`sr_detector.enabled: true`, `config/system_config.yaml` L509). FACT: after each placement and the signal's `PROCESSED` write, A06's `_sr_observe` hands a `Candidate` to `SRDetector.observe`, which enqueues it without waiting; one worker fetches the daily, 60-minute and 30-minute series (180 days), scores the zones, computes the flags (`BUYING_INTO_RESISTANCE`, `SELLING_INTO_SUPPORT`, `LOW_CONFIDENCE_STRUCTURE`, `WEAK_BREAKOUT`, `NO_VOLUME_CONFIRMATION`, `NO_CLEAR_STRUCTURE`) and a V1 retest proposal, and writes one `sr_detector_results` row; the end-of-day backfill (declared at 15:58 Monday to Friday) fills the outcome columns for terminal trades and for rows whose signal has no trade (P03-S101, P03-S103, P03-S030, P03-S043, P02-S040 row 1, P03-S073, P03-S081, P03-S118, P03-S133). INTERPRETATION: by its own code the observer takes no part in the placement it observes; its worker shares the client, the limiter, the database write lock, interpreter time and the instrument cache with the order path (P03-S111). FACT: the intraday anchors (VWAP and opening range) are off (`intraday_anchors_enabled: false`, L542) (P03-S109).
2. **The V2 retest path (section 6) — not built at the committed values** (`wait_for_retest_enabled: false`, L529). FACT: when built, the diverter parks a screened signal whose entry lies in a zone of the configured confidence (committed `HIGH`) on its side, the band widened by `near_zone_buffer_pct`, before sizing; the monitor polls one-minute candles (committed every 20 s) and, on a break, a retest and a strong close, calls A06's `continue_from_retest`, which places a MARKET entry with a structure stop from the zone band and `sl_buffer_pct` (P04-S036, P04-S038, P04-S039, P06-S042, P04-S062, P04-S026, P04-S069, P06-S096, P04-S096, P04-S100). FACT and INTERPRETATION: at the baseline wiring the monitor subtracts a naive divert time from an aware clock, which raises `TypeError` at every evaluation, so with the switch on no parked candidate would be confirmed, rejected or timed out (P04-S070). INTERPRETATION: rehydration restores every row whatever its date, and the end-of-day clear writes no status (P04-S081, P04-S087).
3. **Zone supply to the structure exit (sections 4, 9 and 11) — not built at the committed values** (`structure_exit_enabled: false`, L634). FACT: the zone cache and the warmer are built when the retest path or the structure exit is switched on; A11's structure exit reads the cached zones and A08's `_strong_close`; the warmer is fed only from signal arrivals and its own re-warm scan (P06-S088, P06-S097, P02-S159). INTERPRETATION: zones exist only for symbols that had a signal since the process started (P02-S159; P06-S091).

**The scripts (sections 5 and 7).**

- INTERPRETATION: the backfill's declared run handles only its own day's rows, so a trade still open at 15:58 is revisited only by a run given that date, and a manual run on a weekend or listed holiday is skipped by the cron-entry guard (P03-S135, P03-S131).
- FACT: the corporate-action spot check builds its own client from the token file (recorded at the interface only), prints a verdict by a fixed 0.15 rule over any consecutive pair of daily closes, and records nothing; no document or commit message at the baseline records a run or a result (P05-S021, P05-S030, P05-S031, P05-S034, P05-S040 to P05-S045); whether it ever ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S046). INTERPRETATION (by reading only; nothing run): the level export cannot produce its CSV at the baseline; it stops at an import, if one fails in its environment, or else at L139 (`TypeError` inside `load_all`), before the L141 that A01 C38b records (P05-S059; P05-S070, P05-S082).

**What is built at the committed values (section 9).** INTERPRETATION: switch row 2: the V1 observer only, beside A18's V3 chain (`"shadow"`) and PB-01 watchlist (`true`), which reuse A08's zone builder and fetchers; three `OhlcFetcher` objects over the closure; no zone cache, warmer, monitor or diverter (P06-S090). FACT: the file differs from the loader defaults for one key only, `enabled: true` (P06-S065).

**Consumers (section 11).** INTERPRETATION: at the committed values A08 supplies no technical context to any live entry, risk or exit decision; the V2 path (entry), its resumption (A09's sizing, A10's placement) and A11's structure exit (exit) would, under their switches; A18's consumers reuse A08's functions in shadow (P06-S090, P06-S093, P06-S095 to P06-S097, P06-S101 to P06-S104; P04-S017 (c): the PB-01 entry stage's docstring, "This stage holds NO order/reserve/place capability of any kind", and its own recorder).

**Records (sections 13 to 16).** FACT: the 48 inherited items, the 16 open issues, the 16 Owner-field records and the records that touch A08 code without naming A08 are recorded at A08's side, with every ID, classification, treatment and status unchanged; Rama's two coverage decisions of 26-Nov-2026 and Claude's assignment decisions are flagged for ChatGPT's STUDY review (sections 13.4 and 13.6). The STUDY carries 92 points for the AUDIT, 40 unknown items and 25 draft questions for Rama, from 104, 61 and 27 in the parts (sections 15 and 16).

## 3. The calculation core: data model, pivots, zones and confluence scoring

This section covers the four calculation-core files of the S&R package: `sr_detector/models.py` (the value objects, `Candle.from_kite` and the module constants), `sr_detector/pivots.py` (`find_swing_pivots`), `sr_detector/zones.py` (`cluster_zones`, `_zone_from_cluster`, `volume_profile_nodes`) and `sr_detector/confluence.py` (`ConfluenceContext`, `ScoringParams`, `score_zones` and its helpers). Their callers are recorded at the interface only (D-41, P3); the zone builder, the fetcher, the detector, the flags and the V2 objects that call them are described in sections 4 to 6.

Evidence: `A08_PIVOTS_ZONES_AND_CONFLUENCE_SCORING_2026-11-26.md` (P01), which read the four files in full at the baseline and every caller at the interface; no legacy code was run. Where a statement describes what the code would do with inputs it was never shown to receive, it rests on the code lines and on current external context (the Python documentation pages read on 26-Nov-2026 and a plain-expression check on the work environment's own interpreter, P01-S004, P01-S005, P01-S133) and is labelled INTERPRETATION (P01-S006).

**The four files and what they declare.**

- FACT and HISTORICAL (the commit history): `models.py` 207 lines, `pivots.py` 55, `zones.py` 116, `confluence.py` 211; `pivots.py`, `zones.py` and `confluence.py` each have one commit (`dea336ae71c353fd86331a37d0a788e02968aca9`, 2026-06-27), `models.py` that commit and `536172cfadfdd28ec9e5f8c00f9d549f0cd57dc2` (2026-07-12), which added only the confidence-class constants, `TF_ROLE` and three `SRAnalysis` fields; all four are identical at `7d4970a` (P01-S007, P01-S131).
- FACT: the four files import only the standard library and `sr_detector.models`; none reads the clock, logs, performs I/O, mutates a module-level object or mutates its arguments; the only reference-time input is the `now` that a caller places in `ConfluenceContext`; `field` is imported in `confluence.py` L29 and not used (P01-S009, P01-S010).
- HISTORICAL: the module docstrings declare "Pure data — no behaviour, no I/O, stdlib only" (`models.py`) and the locked design decisions SR-P1, SR-P2 (`pivots.py`), SR-Z1 to SR-Z4 (`zones.py`) and SR-C1 to SR-C3 (`confluence.py`, "Confidence is NEVER a single method — it is a weighted sum of independent votes"); `confluence.py` and `models.py` cite a "spec E" and a test cites a "spec K", and no specification file of the S&R detector is tracked at the baseline (P01-S011, P01-S013). INTERPRETATION: "stdlib only" holds read as "no dependency outside the standard library and the package"; "no behaviour" is not literal, since `Zone`, `ScoredZone` and `Candidate` carry small properties and methods and `from_kite` converts values (P01-S012).

**`Candle` and `Candle.from_kite` (A05 CM-10, HA-08, HA-15; section 13).**

- FACT: `Candle` is a frozen dataclass of six fields (`ts`, `open`, `high`, `low`, `close`, `volume`) with no validation and no field for the instrument, the interval, the source or whether the bar is complete; its docstring says `ts` is "timezone-aware (or naive IST)" (P01-S014). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: what the broker's historical `volume` meant in the legacy period; the vendor page does not define it and A05 keeps it UNKNOWN; a legacy document describes per-interval volume (HISTORICAL) (P01-S014, P01-S138; P01-U10).
- FACT: `from_kite` (L33 to L43) reads exactly `date`, `open`, `high`, `low`, `close` by subscription and `volume` by `.get`, converting the prices with `float()` and the volume with `int(row.get("volume") or 0)`; it has no `try`, logs nothing, rounds nothing and reads no tick size; `row["date"]` is stored unchanged, with no parsing or time-zone handling (P01-S015, P01-S016). INTERPRETATION (A05 STUDY section 4, the published `kiteconnect` source): with that library the value is the `datetime` the library parsed, aware when the server's string carried an offset and naive otherwise; whether the legacy server sent aware times throughout is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P01-S016; P01-U04).
- INTERPRETATION (Python semantics, P01-S004, P01-S005): a missing price key raises `KeyError`, a `None` price `TypeError`, a non-numeric string `ValueError`; NaN and infinity are accepted and stored; no relation between the values is checked (`high < low`, a close outside the range, zero or negative prices all pass) (P01-S017). A missing, `None`, zero or empty volume becomes 0, a fractional one is truncated, `"12.5"` or a NaN volume raises `ValueError`, an infinite one `OverflowError`, and a negative one passes; a consumer cannot tell a missing volume from a zero one (P01-S018).
- FACT: `from_kite` raises to its caller; in `sr_detector/fetch.py` L126 the conversion is inside the `try` of `_fetch_one`, so an exception from any row makes the whole interval's result `None` with one WARNING (section 4); A18's `scripts/forward_shadow_record.py` L212 swallows it (P01-S019). INTERPRETATION: one malformed row discards the whole list for that symbol and interval (P01-S019). FACT: A05's `resample` builds `Candle` objects directly at `core/candle_math.py` L427, so the conversions apply only to candles made by `from_kite` (P01-S020).
- FACT: `from_kite` converts one row and neither sorts nor de-duplicates; `fetch.py` converts the rows in the order the closure returned them (P01-S021). Whether the legacy server's rows were always ascending and unique is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (A05 CM-05; P01-S021).

**The other value objects and constants.**

- FACT: `Pivot` (`price`, `ts`, `kind`, `bar_index`); `bar_index` is read by no code (P01-S022). `Zone` (`band_low`, `band_high`, `kind`, `touches`, `timeframe`, `last_touch_ts`) with `center`, `width` and `contains`; nothing checks `band_low <= band_high` or the kind (P01-S023). `contains` widens the band by `buffer_pct` per cent of the band's centre, inclusive at both ends (P01-S024; the unit reading is INTERPRETATION). `Zone.width` and `Zone.contains` have no caller, in the code or the tests (P01-S025).
- FACT: `ScoredZone` (`band_low`, `band_high`, `kind`, `score`, `confidence`, `touches`, `timeframes`, `evidence`, `last_touch_ts`) has `center`, `contains` (the same rule as `Zone.contains`; its one non-test caller is the retest divert, `screening/retest_monitor.py` L386) and `to_dict`, which returns eight keys, rounds the band edges, the score and the evidence contributions to four decimals and omits `last_touch_ts` (P01-S026 to P01-S028). INTERPRETATION: `round` is half-to-even on binary floats, so a stored value and the in-memory object can differ below the fourth decimal (P01-S028). The consumers of `to_dict` are the detector, the retest divert's audit row and three A18 V3 files; the zone builder's per-timeframe payload builds its own rounded dict (P01-S029).
- FACT: `Candidate` (the snapshot of a placed entry handed to the observer) validates nothing; `is_long` is `direction.strip().upper() in {"LONG", "BUY"}` (P01-S030, P01-S031). INTERPRETATION: every other value counts as the short side and a `None` direction raises `AttributeError` (P01-S031). FACT: its builders are A06's `_sr_observe` (`signals/signal_processor.py` L640 to L654, called at L1550, L2296 and L2588) and the export script (L167 to L172); the docstring's "at _emit_signal_alert" names a method that exists but is not the builder (P01-S032).
- FACT: `RetestProposal` (`would_wait`, `entry`, `sl`), "V1 logs the PROPOSAL only" (P01-S033); the structure-status constants `OK`, `NO_CLEAR_STRUCTURE`, `FETCH_FAILED` (P01-S034); `TF_ROLE` maps the five intervals the loader allows to role labels (P01-S036); `SRAnalysis` is the detector's verdict object, with three `dict` fields that stay mutable inside the frozen dataclass (P01-S037; the last point INTERPRETATION).
- FACT and HISTORICAL: `CONF_ANCHOR_ONLY` and `CONF_ANCHOR_PLUS_VALIDATED_SWINGS` (L175, L176); the comment L169 to L174 says swings are "flagged NOT-YET-VALIDATED until Rama's manual-marking validation passes" and that "The 03.03 R:R gate will use ANCHOR-only levels for live capital until then"; the validated class has no non-test user (P01-S035). INTERPRETATION: no code assigns the validated class, so every `SRAnalysis` and V3 record carries `ANCHOR_ONLY`, and nothing in the four files restricts which zones a consumer may use because of it (P01-S035).

**Swing pivots (`find_swing_pivots`).**

- FACT: the function returns no pivots when `left < 1`, `right < 1` or there are fewer than `left + right + 1` candles, and checks nothing else (P01-S038, P01-S039). For each index from `left` to `n - right - 1` it takes the window of `left + right + 1` bars; a HIGH pivot needs the bar's high to be at least every high in the window and strictly above at least one other bar's (SR-P1); LOW is the mirror; only `high` and `low` are read (P01-S040, P01-S041). INTERPRETATION: the first `left` and last `right` bars are never examined, as SR-P2 states (P01-S040). FACT: pivots are appended in increasing position, HIGH before LOW at one bar; `bar_index` is the list position, not a time (P01-S044). The function catches nothing and logs nothing (P01-S049).
- INTERPRETATION: every bar of a plateau with lower bars around it becomes a pivot at the same price (highs `[1, 2, 5, 5, 2, 1]` with `n = 2` give two HIGH pivots), and an outside bar yields both a HIGH and a LOW pivot, which the docstring's "only in degenerate single-bar windows" does not describe (P01-S042, P01-S043).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (input order): the function does not sort and treats list position as time; with the equal `left` and `right` that every non-test caller passes, a reversed series gives the same pivot bars in reversed output order, and any other order gives pivots from positional neighbours that are not time neighbours, with no error; a consumer that reads the output order as time is affected even by a reversed series (P01-S045, P01-S119). INTERPRETATION: a repeated bar can create two pivots and two touches; a missing bar is not detected (P01-S046). A NaN high suppresses every HIGH pivot in a window that contains it, an infinite high can become a pivot, and nothing raises; a non-integer `n` would raise `TypeError`, and every non-test caller passes an integer (P01-S047).
- INTERPRETATION: with the committed `default_pivot_n: 5` (`config/system_config.yaml` L513; committed value only) the newest possible pivot is five bars before the last bar given, on the daily series and on the 60-minute and 30-minute series alike; a still-forming last bar can be a neighbour but not a pivot (P01-S048; P01-S096).

**Zone clustering and the volume profile (`zones.py`).**

- FACT: `cluster_zones` takes the pivots of one timeframe, handles HIGH (to RESISTANCE) then LOW (to SUPPORT), sorts each kind by price and joins a pivot to the running cluster when its price is at most `cluster_pct` per cent above the cluster's highest price; otherwise it closes the cluster; a pivot of another kind is ignored without a log (P01-S050 to P01-S052). `_zone_from_cluster` makes the band `[min, max]` of the cluster's prices, `touches` the number of pivots and `last_touch_ts` the latest pivot `ts` (SR-Z2) (P01-S057).
- INTERPRETATION: because the pivots are sorted, the join test is against the previous pivot, so membership chains without a width limit (pivots 0.4 apart at 100.0 to 102.0 form one zone 2 per cent wide with `cluster_pct` 0.5), as SR-Z1 states (P01-S053). There is no minimum number of touches: a lone pivot is a zone of zero width, against the `Zone` docstring "A price BAND (never a single line)"; only the two buffered membership checks widen a band, for that check only (P01-S054). `touches` counts pivots, not distinct swings, so a plateau, a duplicated bar or an outside bar adds touches (P01-S056). `cluster_pct` of zero or below is not rejected; committed `cluster_pct: 0.5` equals the loader and builder fallbacks (committed value only) (P01-S055). A cluster mixing naive and aware `ts` values would raise `TypeError` in `max`; within one fetched series a mix is not expected (P01-S058).
- FACT: `volume_profile_nodes` buckets each bar's whole volume at its mid-range price into `bins` equal-width buckets over `[min low, max high]` and returns the centres of the buckets whose volume is at least `node_frac` times the largest; it returns nothing for no candles, `bins < 1`, `hi <= lo` or a non-positive peak (P01-S059 to P01-S063). INTERPRETATION: nodes are bucket centres, not traded prices; adjacent nodes are not merged; a series of zero volumes (for example volumes defaulted to 0 by `from_kite`) gives no nodes (P01-S062, P01-S063, P01-S099). A NaN price, a `+inf` high or a `-inf` low raises `ValueError` here, and an infinity on the wrong side of an invalid bar `OverflowError`, uncaught (P01-S064). FACT: `volume_bins` and `volume_node_frac` are not in the committed file, so the loader defaults 24 and 0.7 apply (P01-S065); the one non-test caller passes the daily series when one is present, otherwise the first series in the dict (P01-S066; the choice is the zone builder's, section 4).

**Confluence scoring (`confluence.py`).**

- FACT: `ConfluenceContext` carries the prior-day levels, the volume nodes, `now` and `recency_window_days` (90.0); `ScoringParams` carries six weights, `t_high` 5.0, `t_med` 3.0, `touch_cap` 4, `merge_pct` 0.4, `band_buffer_pct` and `recency_min_factor`; neither validates its values (P01-S067, P01-S068). The only non-test constructions are in the zone builder (P01-S069). At the four layers (dataclass default, builder fallback, loader default, committed file) every value agrees except `band_buffer_pct`: 0.0 in the dataclass, 0.1 in the builder, the loader and the committed file (L516); `touch_cap`, `recency_min_factor` and `recency_window_days` are not in the committed file (P01-S069). INTERPRETATION: the dataclass defaults apply only where `ScoringParams` is built directly, as the unit tests do (P01-S069).
- FACT: `score_zones` merges each kind across the timeframes and returns every group, RESISTANCE then SUPPORT, each in ascending centre; nothing is filtered or sorted by score (P01-S070). `_merge_across_tfs` sorts the zones of one kind from all timeframes by centre and joins a zone to the current group when its centre is within `merge_pct` per cent of the group's running mean centre (P01-S071). INTERPRETATION: the tolerance compares centres, not band edges; the mean drifts upward as members join; and zones of one timeframe can join one group, so SR-C1's "ACROSS timeframes" describes the purpose rather than a rule; the merged band is the union, gaps included (P01-S072 to P01-S074).
- FACT: `_score_group` appends up to six votes in a fixed order: `swing_pivots` (`w_swing × min(touches, touch_cap)`), `multi_tf` (`w_multi_tf ×` the number of distinct timeframe labels), `volume_node` and `prior_day_level` (each when a level falls in the band widened by `band_buffer_pct`), `round_number` (no buffer) and `recency`; the score is their sum and the bucket is HIGH when `score >= t_high`, at least two distinct method types and at least two timeframes, MEDIUM when `score >= t_med`, else LOW (P01-S075, P01-S076, P01-S080). The formula is written out in P01-S077; with the three committed timeframes a group scores between 2 and 10 (INTERPRETATION) (P01-S077).
- INTERPRETATION (the committed weights): `swing_pivots` and `multi_tf` are always appended, so the "≥2 distinct method-types" condition is always met; every group present on all three timeframes is HIGH, a two-timeframe group is HIGH from three summed touches, a single-timeframe group can never be HIGH, and a lone pivot is LOW (or MEDIUM only with a round number and a full recency factor) — all without any volume, prior-day, round-number or recency vote (P01-S078, P01-S079, P01-S121). The volume, prior-day and round-number votes are recorded and counted as method types even at weight 0 (latent at the committed weights) (P01-S081). The score is compared unrounded; `to_dict` rounds it for storage (P01-S082).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (the two band tests): `_level_in_band` uses the same inclusive, centre-relative buffer as `contains` (P01-S083). `_has_round_number` tests whether the band contains a multiple of the power of ten below its centre or of half of it; INTERPRETATION: the first step adds nothing, so the rule is in effect "the band contains a multiple of half the power of ten below its centre", whose step jumps tenfold at each power of ten (multiples of 5 for a centre of 99.9, of 50 for 100.1), between about 5 and 50 per cent of price (P01-S084, P01-S085). A NaN or `+inf` centre raises `ValueError` or `OverflowError`, uncaught (P01-S086). A second, related rule (`round_number_levels`) is in the zone builder, for the anchor payload (P01-S087).
- FACT: `_recency_factor` returns 0.0 for no touch time, 1.0 when `now` is `None` or the window is non-positive, strips both time zones when exactly one side is naive, and otherwise returns `1 − age_days / window`, clamped to at most 1.0 and at least `recency_min_factor` (P01-S088). INTERPRETATION: the decay is linear in calendar days (weekends and holidays included); a missing clock gives every dated zone the full recency vote; the stripped comparison gives the elapsed time only when both wall clocks are IST; a `recency_min_factor` above 1 would escape the upper clamp (P01-S089 to P01-S091).
- INTERPRETATION: the four files are deterministic functions of their inputs; output depends on input order only through the pivot positions (P01-S092). FACT: zones come out by ascending centre, not by score or distance; the detector logs the first twelve of each kind in that order (loader default `max_zones_logged` 12); the consumers that choose a zone by price use their own key (P01-S093). INTERPRETATION: with more zones the lowest-priced are kept in the log, not the strongest or the nearest (the slicing is the detector's logic, section 5) (P01-S093).

**Data quality across the four files (summary; A05 CM-05, CM-10, HA-04, HA-08).**

- INTERPRETATION: nothing from `from_kite` to `score_zones` orders candles by time; only the pivots depend on order (P01-S094). A duplicated bar can add touches and doubles its volume in the profile; gaps are not detected; nothing de-duplicates by `ts` (P01-S095). A forming last bar cannot be a pivot but can decide its neighbours and adds partial volume (P01-S096). Bad values: `from_kite` raises on missing or non-numeric prices and accepts NaN and infinity; the pivots silently skip NaN windows; the volume profile and the round-number test raise on NaN or infinities; mixed naive and aware times raise in two `max` calls but not in the recency factor; none of the four files checks `high >= low`, positive prices or non-negative volume, and none catches an exception (P01-S097). Zero and negative prices make the percentage tolerances zero or negative (P01-S098). A series of fewer than 11 bars at `n = 5` gives no pivots (P01-S100). Every tolerance is a percentage of a price; none uses ATR, a tick size or an absolute amount; the pivot window counts bars, the recency window calendar days (P01-S101). Whether any such value reached the code in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P01-U02, P01-U03).

**Callers (interface only; D-41, P3).**

- FACT: inside A08, the one zone-building path is `scored_zones_from_candles` in `sr_detector/zone_builder.py` L85 to L107, which runs `find_swing_pivots`, `cluster_zones`, `volume_profile_nodes`, `prior_day_levels` and `score_zones` with no `try`; the V1 detector and the zone warmer pass `_safe_now()` as `now` (`None` if the clock raises); the flags read `is_long`, `center`, the HIGH label and the band edges; the retest divert calls `ScoredZone.contains` with the configured confidence filter (committed `HIGH`) and picks the nearest centre (P01-S102 to P01-S107). The package re-exports `Candidate`, which A06 imports from the package (P01-S134).
- FACT (other topics' files): the regime engine (A18) runs `find_swing_pivots` on the index's daily series with `n` 3 (loader default) and reads `highs[-1]`, `highs[-2]` as time order; the V3 runner and the PB-01 runner (A18) call `scored_zones_from_candles` on as-of-truncated series inside a `try` and run `find_swing_pivots` on the 60-minute series for `gate_htf` (A07's function, A18's use), whose docstring calls the lists "chronological"; the PB-01 watchlist capture fetches only 60-minute and 30-minute bars; `core/candle_math.py` (A05) and `core/daily_stats.py` (A15) use the `Candle` type; `scripts/forward_shadow_record.py` (A18) calls `from_kite`; A11's structure exit reads the cached zones' fields; A15's `reports/daily_trade_review.py` reads the stored zone keys; `main.py` calls no function of the four files, and for them imports only the zone builder's two knob builders (P01-S108 to P01-S118, P01-S135, P01-S136).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (the clocks reaching `now`): the detector, the warmer and the watchlist capture pass an aware IST clock or `None`; the two V3 runners pass their `as_of`; the export script passes a naive 15:30 `as_of` for the chosen date (P01-S137, P01-S119). Which of these paths ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P01-S119; P01-U05).

The intent sources (the locked design decisions, the comments, the tests and the legacy documents) are compared with the code in section 12; the points for the AUDIT are P01-AP01 to P01-AP17 (section 15.1), the unknown items P01-U01 to P01-U10 (section 15.2) and the questions P01-R1 to P01-R4 (section 16).

## 4. Candle fetch, zone building, the zone cache and the zone warmer

This section covers `sr_detector/fetch.py` (`OhlcFetcher`), `sr_detector/zone_builder.py` (the knobs, the parameter builders, prior-day levels, the zone-building path, round numbers, intraday anchors and the two payloads), `sr_detector/zone_cache.py` (`ZoneSet`, `ZoneCache`) and `sr_detector/zone_warmer.py` (`ZoneWarmer`). The shared fetch closure in `main.py` and the consumers in other topics' files are recorded at the interface only (D-41, P3).

Evidence: `A08_CANDLE_FETCH_ZONE_BUILDER_CACHE_AND_WARMER_2026-11-26.md` (P02), which read the four files in full and every caller at the interface. External behaviour (the Kite Connect "Historical candle data" page; the Python `queue`, `threading`, `time`, `datetime` and built-in pages; the pydantic `BaseModel` page) is current external context read on 26-Nov-2026 (P02-S004, P02-S005, P02-S160).

**The four files.**

- FACT: `fetch.py` 159 lines, `zone_builder.py` 224, `zone_cache.py` 76, `zone_warmer.py` 161 (P02-S007). None reads the system clock directly: every time value comes from an injected `now_fn` or a `now` argument, in every trading-process construction `time_authority.now_ist` (the wall clock in IST) (P02-S009, P02-S093). `fetch.py` imports nothing from `broker/` and receives the broker call as an argument (SR-F1) (P02-S008, P02-S011).
- HISTORICAL: `fetch.py` declares "Detector-side fetch orchestration: resolve a symbol → instrument_token, fetch the 3 timeframe windows (one request each), cache within the session, and FAIL SAFE (never raise; an error yields no candles so the detector logs a fetch_failed row and skips analysis — spec C)", with SR-F1 to SR-F4; `zone_builder.py` "The SINGLE zone-building path, shared by the V1 detector (async observer) and the V2 ZoneWarmer" (P02-S010, P02-S142, P02-S147).

**`OhlcFetcher`: the injected closure, the methods and the window.**

- FACT: every non-test construction passes the one closure `_sr_fetch_fn`, built once at `main.py` L3479 to L3487 when any of six switches is on (`_v1_on`, `_v2_on`, `_struct_exit_on`, `_regime_on`, `_v3_chain_on`, `_watchlist_on`); the class calls it positionally, once per cache miss, never while holding its lock, and never retries (P02-S012 to P02-S014).
- FACT: `fetch_timeframes` resolves the token (`None` gives `{}`) and fetches each interval with the constructor's lookback, adding an interval only when its result is non-empty; `fetch_interval` fetches one interval with a lookback of its own; `fetch_by_token` returns `None` for a falsy token without a log line and keys the cache by `cache_key` or `__token_<n>` (P02-S015, P02-S017, P02-S018). INTERPRETATION: a failed interval and an empty one are both simply absent, so a caller cannot tell from the result which intervals failed (P02-S016).
- FACT: `_resolve_token` reads `instrument_token` from the instrument cache and returns `token or None`, logging a WARNING only on an exception (P02-S020). INTERPRETATION: a missing symbol, a row without the attribute and a `None` token are logged and give no candles; a token of 0 gives no candles silently (A05 IN-02) (P02-S021).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION: the window runs from `now − lb` calendar days to `now`, with `now` the injected clock at the moment of the miss; no market hours, session or trading calendar is used (P02-S022, P02-S023). INTERPRETATION and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the fetcher applies no as-of truncation and no completeness test, so a still-forming last candle, if the broker sent one, is converted, cached and returned as received; whether it did is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (A05 HA-04) (P02-S024).
- FACT: rows are converted with `Candle.from_kite` in the order the closure returned them, inside the `try`, so one unconvertible row discards the interval (P02-S025; P01-S019). No line of the four files sorts candles, removes duplicates, detects gaps, checks that timestamps increase or checks values (A05 CM-05) (P02-S026). An empty or `None` answer is returned as `None`, not cached and not logged (P02-S027).

**The fetcher's cache.**

- FACT: the key is `(symbol or cache key, interval)` with no lookback or window, and the value is the injected clock's reading and the list (P02-S028). The age is the difference of two `now_fn` readings, the wall clock in IST, while the field comment L49 says "fetched_at_monotonic_dt" (P02-S029, P02-S144). The test is `age > ttl`, so an entry exactly as old as the TTL is served; the PB-01 and one-minute fetchers are built with a TTL of 0 (P02-S031). Nothing is ever evicted, and there is no size limit (P02-S032). A hit returns the stored list object itself (P02-S033). One `threading.Lock` is held only for the dictionary read and write (P02-S034).
- INTERPRETATION: a backward clock step makes an entry live longer than its TTL and a forward step expires it early; ages run through nights and holidays (P02-S030). A TTL of 0 still stores every list and serves it only at a zero or negative age (P02-S031). Every caller of one object within the TTL receives the same mutable list (P02-S033). Two threads that miss on one key both fetch; there is no single-flight (P02-S034).
- INTERPRETATION (A05 HA-06; note N2): because the key has no window, a second request for one key with a different window inside the TTL receives the first request's list; no committed construction makes such a pair; with `intraday_anchors_enabled: true` and `anchor_intraday_interval: 30minute` (a value the loader accepts) a failed structural `30minute` fetch followed by a successful one-day anchor fetch would give later structural requests one day of bars as their 180-day series, with no log line; on the export script's per-call fetcher, whose clock is constant, the anchor request receives the structural list when the command's interval is a structure interval (P02-S042, P02-S160). Whether either occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S042).

**Failures, logging, retries and the limiter (A04 RL-05, A05 HA-01).**

- FACT: one `except Exception` covers the clock read, the window, the closure (the limiter and the broker call), every row's conversion and `_cache_put` (P02-S035). The file writes two log lines only, WARNING "token lookup failed" and WARNING "historical_data failed", the second for every exception, including a limiter timeout or shutdown abort in which no broker call was made, a broker or library error (a 429 among them), a conversion failure and a cache-write failure; there is no 429 path, no `penalize` and no counter (P02-S037, P02-S038, P02-S104, P02-S107). The shutdown abort is logged at WARNING while its class's docstring says "INFO (not WARN/ERROR)" (P02-S107).
- INTERPRETATION: two paths that a non-test caller can reach raise to the caller despite "never raise": a clock failure in `_cache_get` (outside the `try`) and a token that `int` cannot convert in `fetch_by_token`; argument types that no non-test caller passes (an unhashable key, an `intervals` value that cannot be iterated) would also raise outside the `try`; a `_cache_put` failure after a successful broker call is reported as "historical_data failed" and the candles are discarded (P02-S019, P02-S036). FACT: the four files contain no retry, backoff or sleep on the fetch path; the only pacing is the closure's `acquire("historical")` (A04's), and a failed interval is asked again only by the next miss (P02-S039).

**The seven constructions (P02-S040; committed values only).**

- FACT: (1) the V1 detector's fetcher, built inside `build_sr_detector` (`sr_detector/__init__.py` L53 to L60), 180 days, TTL 1800 s, thread "sr-detector", under `sr_detector.enabled` (committed `true`); (2) the market regime's (A18), under `regime.enabled` (`false`); (3) the structure fetcher for the zone warmer, 180 days, TTL 1800 s, thread "sr-zone-warmer", under `_v2_on or _struct_exit_on` (both `false`); (4) the V3 chain's (A18), under `v3_chain_mode != "off"` (committed `"shadow"`); (5) the PB-01 watchlist's (A18), one object shared by three A18 stages, TTL 0, under `watchlist.enabled` (`true`); (6) the retest monitor's one-minute fetcher, two days, TTL 0, thread "sr-retest-monitor", inside the V2 block; (7) the export script's, one per analysis, in its own process over its own closure and limiter, with a constant naive clock (P02-S040). INTERPRETATION: at the committed values the trading process would build rows 1, 4 and 5, and only row 1's consumer is A08's; each object has its own cache; they share only the closure and, through it, the limiter's `historical` bucket and the client; which objects existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S041).
- FACT: `effect_telemetry.register_constructed("ohlc_fetchers")` follows the five constructions in `main.py`; the V1 detector's fetcher, built inside the package, has no such call (A05 XC-03's fetcher-count point; P02-S099).

**`zone_builder`: knobs, parameters and the one zone-building path.**

- FACT: `_attr` is `getattr(cfg, name, default)`, so a fallback applies only where the object lacks the attribute (P02-S043). `ZoneKnobs` (seven fields, no defaults), `build_zone_knobs`, `build_scoring_params` (twelve fields, cast; the one builder fallback that differs from the dataclass default is `band_buffer_pct`, 0.1 against 0.0) and `build_flag_params` (`entry_proximity_pct` 1.0, `volume_surge_mult` 1.5, `weak_breakout_frac` 0.25, `retest_sl_buffer_pct` 0.3) read the configuration object; the values at each layer are in P02-S045 and P01-S069 (P02-S044 to P02-S047).
- FACT: `prior_day_levels` returns the high, low and close of `daily[-2]` (or `daily[-1]` for a one-candle list), by list position only (P02-S048). INTERPRETATION: that is the previous trading day only when the series ends with a candle for today, and otherwise the day before; a descending or duplicated series gives other candles; whether the broker included today's candle is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S048).
- FACT: `scored_zones_from_candles` runs, for each interval, `find_swing_pivots` with `pivot_n_by_tf.get(tf, default_pivot_n)` and `cluster_zones`, then builds the `ConfluenceContext` from the daily series (or the first series) and calls `score_zones`; every non-test production of scored zones (the V1 detector, the warmer, the V3 runner, the PB-01 runner and the watchlist capture) goes through it (P02-S049, P02-S051). INTERPRETATION: with no daily series the "prior-day levels" and the volume nodes come from the first intraday series; an empty dictionary raises `StopIteration`, which no non-test caller passes (P02-S050). FACT: `split_zones` splits by kind, keeping the input order (P02-S052).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION: `round_number_levels` lists the multiples of the power of ten below a price and of its half, below and above, rounded to six decimals, and returns `()` for a missing or non-positive price; a NaN or `+inf` price raises, uncaught, against the docstring (P02-S053, P02-S054). `intraday_anchors` keeps the fine candles whose label's wall-clock date is the candidate's date and whose wall-clock time is in `[session_open, session_close)`, computes A05's `session_vwap` on a non-empty scope and the opening-range high and low over the first `orb_window_minutes` (P02-S055). INTERPRETATION: the scoping fits labels that mark the start of a bar in IST wall-clock time; there is no completeness rule, so a call during the opening window or with a forming bar uses partial data (P02-S056 to P02-S058). FACT: `build_anchor_payload` and `build_swings_payload` build the two evidence payloads; INTERPRETATION: without a daily series the anchor payload's `prior_day` is empty while the confluence context uses the first series; the swing payload lists the merged group's values under each contributing role (P02-S059 to P02-S062).

**`ZoneCache`.**

- FACT: `ZoneSet` holds the resistance and support tuples and `warmed_at`, and records no symbol, intervals, candle age or knobs (P02-S063). The one construction (`main.py` L3557 to L3558) passes `sr_detector.zone_cache_ttl_sec` (committed 1800) and `now_ist`; one `RLock` guards the dictionary (P02-S064, P02-S071). `put` replaces the symbol's entry with `warmed_at` = the time of the `put`; `get` returns `None` for no entry or an age above the TTL (P02-S065, P02-S066). `symbols_due_for_rewarm` returns every entry whose age is at least `ttl − margin`, with no upper bound (P02-S067). `evict_expired`, `size` and `clear` have no non-test caller, and nothing clears the store during the object's life; the end-of-day square-off clears the retest monitor's parked candidates, not the zone cache (P02-S068, P02-S073).
- INTERPRETATION: the age runs from the `put`, not from the candles' fetch, so at the committed values a `ZoneSet` reported at most about 1500 s old can rest on candles about 3000 s old (about 3600 s at the extreme); a reader cannot see this, nor tell a never-warmed symbol from an expired one, nor a set built from three intervals from one built from one (P02-S069, P02-S070, P02-S072).

**`ZoneWarmer`.**

- FACT: the one construction (`main.py` L3564 to L3568, inside `if _v2_on or _struct_exit_on:` and a `try` that sets the warmer and the cache to `None` on failure) passes no `idle_sleep_sec`, `max_queue` or `enabled`, so 2.0 s, 512 and `True` apply; no configuration key reaches the queue size, the idle sleep or the enable flag, and `sr_detector.max_queue` (loader 256) feeds only the V1 detector's own queue (A02 K-23) (P02-S074 to P02-S076, P02-S102). `start` returns without a log line when disabled or already started and otherwise starts the daemon thread "sr-zone-warmer"; it is called at `main.py` L3796 (V2 block) or L3816 (structure-exit block) (P02-S077, P02-S078).
- FACT (A06 WD-10, PQ-11; note N3): `enqueue` returns at once when disabled or for a falsy symbol; it deduplicates under `_pending_lock`, then calls `put_nowait`; on a full queue it drops the symbol with a WARNING and removes it from `_pending`; any other error is logged at ERROR; it makes no fetch, clock read, store call or cache call (P02-S079, P02-S080). INTERPRETATION: the dispatcher's `_warm_zones` call never waits for a fetch, the limiter, the broker or a free queue slot; it can wait only for two short locks and, on the drop and error paths, the logger (P02-S080, P02-S111, P02-S130); beyond these locks the threads share one interpreter, so the warmer's own Python work and the dispatcher take turns (P02-S080; the production interpreter is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, P02-U11). How long those waits took is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S112).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION: `enqueue` has two callers, A06's `_warm_zones` on a signal's arrival and the warmer's own re-warm scan; neither reader of the zone cache enqueues, so a symbol has a `ZoneSet` only after a signal for it has reached the dispatcher in the current process (P02-S159). The dedup covers only queued symbols, so a symbol can be queued again while it is warmed (P02-S081).
- FACT: the worker takes one symbol at a time with a 2.0-s timeout and, when the queue is empty, runs `_enqueue_rewarms`; `warm` fetches the knobs' intervals, logs INFO "fetch miss" and stores nothing on an empty result, and otherwise scores, splits and `put`s, logging INFO "warmed" (P02-S082, P02-S084, P02-S085). INTERPRETATION: a fetch miss keeps the old entry, which stays due; any non-empty result is stored, even with only some intervals or no zone at all (returning `True`, against the docstring "Returns True if zones were cached"); the re-warm scan returns expired entries too, so every symbol warmed once is re-warmed about every 25 minutes (TTL 1800 s minus margin 300 s; committed values) for the life of the process, after the close included, and a symbol whose re-warm gets no interval at all is queued again at every idle scan, about every 2 s (P02-S083, P02-S084, P02-S086).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION: `stop` sets the stop event, posts a sentinel, joins for 2.0 s and clears the worker reference whether or not the join completed (called at `main.py` L1744); a worker inside a broker call is not interrupted and can still write to the cache after the join returns; `enqueue` keeps accepting symbols after `stop` (P02-S088, P02-S089). At the committed values (`wait_for_retest_enabled` and `structure_exit_enabled` both `false`) no warmer, zone cache or structure fetcher is built, and the processor's `_warm_zones` is a no-op (P02-S090, P02-S100).

**Threads, locks, clocks and failure behaviour (summary).** The threads that run the four files' code (P02-S091), the four locks, none taken while another is held and none held during a fetch, a limiter wait, a broker call, a scoring run or a log call (P02-S092), the clocks (wall clock only; no monotonic or market clock; P02-S093) and the outcome of each failure case, with its log line and whether it propagates (P02-S094), are tabled in P02 section 7 and summarised in section 10 below. Failures leave log lines only: the four files write no counter, alert, telemetry or stored record (P02-S094).

The intent sources are compared in section 12; the points for the AUDIT are P02-AP01 to P02-AP17 (section 15.1), the unknown items P02-U01 to P02-U11 (section 15.2) and the questions P02-R1 to P02-R5 (section 16).

## 5. The V1 observer, its flags, the `sr_detector_results` row and the end-of-day backfill

This section covers the V1 S&R observer ("SNR-DETECTOR-V1", D-41 P2): `sr_detector/__init__.py` (`build_sr_detector`), `sr_detector/detector.py` (`SRDetector`), `sr_detector/flags.py` (the flags and the V1 retest proposal), the `sr_detector_results` row it writes, and `scripts/sr_detector_backfill.py`. Its call sites in A06's processor and A01's `main.py`, the store methods and the schema (A03's) and the readers of the table are recorded at the interface only (D-41, P3). A18 later covers only the S&R shadow's activation controls, integration and Live activation status, citing A08 (D-41, P2).

Evidence: `A08_V1_OBSERVER_FLAGS_RESULTS_AND_BACKFILL_2026-11-26.md` (P03), which read the four files in full and every call site at the interface; the SQLite and Python behaviour it relies on is current external context read on 26-Nov-2026 (P03-S005).

**The files and their declared purpose.**

- FACT: `sr_detector/__init__.py` 70 lines, `detector.py` 329, `flags.py` 186, `scripts/sr_detector_backfill.py` 149 (P03-S009, P03-S010).
- HISTORICAL: the package is "A PURE package (imports only core/ + stdlib) that runs as an async, non-gating observer beside order placement", which "never rejects, sizes, or touches SL/TGT/STM, and never blocks/delays placement"; the detector is "The async, NON-GATING observer" with "A single serialized background worker" that does "3 historical fetches → pivots ..."; V1 "answers one question — "can we reliably detect buying-into-resistance before the outcome is known?" — by logging evidence for offline validation"; the flags module emits "zero-or-more structural flags and a RETEST PROPOSAL (proposal only in V1 — no outcome simulation)"; the backfill is the "EOD OUTCOME BACKFILL (spec I) — separate from the live observer" (P03-S012 to P03-S015, P03-S194). INTERPRETATION: the four files import, besides the standard library, `core.time_authority` and `core.effect_telemetry` (and the script `core.state_store` and `utils.cron_heartbeat`), so "imports only core/ + stdlib" holds for the package read as "no dependency outside `core/`, the standard library and the package itself"; `flags.py`'s own "stdlib only" does not, taken literally, cover its import of `sr_detector.models` (P03-S016).

**`build_sr_detector`.**

- FACT: every parameter is keyword-only; with no `now_fn` the default clock is `core.time_authority.now_ist`, an aware IST time; the function builds one `OhlcFetcher` (lookback 180 days and TTL 1800 s by `getattr` defaults) and returns an `SRDetector` with the default version `"snr-v1"`; it reads only `lookback_days` and `cache_ttl_sec` itself and does not read `enabled` (P03-S017 to P03-S021). INTERPRETATION: it contains no `try`, so a construction failure propagates to the caller (P03-S022). FACT: its non-test callers are `main.py` L3493 to L3503 (only when `_v1_on`, inside a `try` that logs and sets the detector to `None`) and the export script, which builds one detector per analysis (P03-S023, P03-S108).

**`SRDetector`: constructor, lifecycle and threading.**

- FACT: the constructor reads the switch `enabled` (code default `False`; committed `true`, `config/system_config.yaml` L509; loader default `False`), `intraday_anchors_enabled`, the anchor keys, `breakout_avg_window`, `max_zones_logged`, `max_queue` and the flag keys, builds the knobs and parameters through the zone builder, and takes one effect-telemetry handle for the unit "sr_detector"; `_enabled` is fixed at construction; the queue is `queue.Queue(maxsize=max_queue)` (loader 256) (P03-S024 to P03-S029).
- FACT: `start` returns at once when disabled or already started and otherwise starts the daemon thread "sr-detector"; `stop` sets the stop event, posts a sentinel (ignoring a full queue), joins for 2.0 s and clears the worker reference in every case, logging nothing; the worker loop takes items with a 0.25-s timeout and runs `process_candidate`, logging any exception at ERROR "worker error" (P03-S030 to P03-S032). The file creates one thread and holds no lock of its own (P03-S034).
- INTERPRETATION: a candidate being processed at `stop` is finished; candidates still queued are dropped without a log line or a row; a worker still inside its fetches after the 2.0-s join is left running, unreported, and is stopped abruptly at interpreter exit; a `start` after a timed-out `stop` would run a second worker, which no caller does (P03-S033). The queue exists only in memory, so candidates waiting in it at a crash or forced kill leave no row and no log line (P03-S216).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (A01 C13): the worker is started at `main.py` L3504 under `_v1_on`, before the `SignalProcessor` is built (L3575); until `observe` hands it a candidate it only waits, making no fetch, write or log line (P03-S035, P03-S036, P03-S176).

**`observe` (A06 EV-23).**

- FACT: `observe` returns at once when disabled; otherwise it calls `put_nowait`, logs a WARNING "queue full, dropping" on `queue.Full` and any other exception through an exception-safe helper (P03-S037). INTERPRETATION: it waits neither for a queue slot nor for the analysis; on the caller's thread it costs the queue's lock and, on a full queue or an unexpected error, one log call; a full queue (256 at the loader default) drops the new candidate with no row; an object without `symbol` would raise in the `Full` handler, and every production caller passes a `Candidate` (P03-S038 to P03-S040, P03-S103, P03-S169). With `_enabled` false no thread is started and `observe` returns at once (P03-S041).

**The per-candidate computation.**

- FACT: `process_candidate` runs `analyze` then `_write`, with no `try` (P03-S042). `analyze` fetches the configured intervals (committed `day`, `60minute`, `30minute`); an empty result returns a `FETCH_FAILED` analysis with the flag `NO_CLEAR_STRUCTURE` and the note "fetch_failed"; otherwise it scores the zones with `now = _safe_now()`, builds the breakout context and the flags, sets `structure_status` to `OK` when any zone exists and `NO_CLEAR_STRUCTURE` otherwise, and, only when the anchors are on, adds the anchors and the swing payload (P03-S043 to P03-S050). INTERPRETATION: the flag fallback at L182 is never taken (P03-S048).
- FACT: `_build_anchors` fetches `anchor_intraday_interval` over `anchor_lookback_days` only when both session bounds are set, inside its own `try` (P03-S051). `_breakout_context` takes the last candle of the 30-minute list (else 60-minute, else daily) and the mean volume of up to `breakout_avg_window` candles before it (P03-S052). `_evidence` stores the `to_dict` of the first `max_zones_logged` (loader 12) zones of each kind, in ascending centre (P03-S054; P01-AP12).
- INTERPRETATION: the "breakout candle" is the last element of the list at processing time, not a candle tied to the entry (P03-S053). An exception from the zone building, the flags, the payloads or `_evidence` leaves `analyze`, skips `_write` and gives one ERROR line, no row and no counter; a fetch failure that the fetcher absorbs gives a `FETCH_FAILED` row instead (P03-S055). When one or two intervals fail, the analysis runs on the others and nothing in the row records which were fetched (P03-S056). The fetch windows end when the worker reaches the candidate, after any queue wait and with cached lists up to 1800 s old, and nothing truncates the candles to the placement-time stamp, so a row can rest partly on price action after the entry; the row stores both `ts` and `created_at`, from which each row's delay can be read (A05 HA-04) (P03-S057). FACT: the detector calls only the fetcher, the zone-builder functions, the flags function, the store's insert, the telemetry counter, the clock and the logger (P03-S058).

**The flags and the V1 retest proposal (`flags.py`).**

- FACT: `BreakoutContext`, `FlagParams` (`entry_proximity_pct` 1.0, `volume_surge_mult` 1.5, `weak_breakout_frac` 0.25, `retest_sl_buffer_pct` 0.3) and `FlagResult` (P03-S059 to P03-S061). The nearest resistance is chosen among the resistance zones whose top reaches the entry (the nearest support among those whose bottom reaches it), by distance of centre, falling back to all zones of the kind when none qualifies; the distances are percentages of the entry to the zone's centre (P03-S062, P03-S063, P03-S065). INTERPRETATION: the recorded "nearest resistance" can lie below the entry, with a negative distance (P03-S064, P03-S065).
- FACT: for a long (`is_long`), the entry is "into" the nearest resistance when it is inside the band or below its bottom by at most `entry_proximity_pct` per cent of the band's centre; the flag is `BUYING_INTO_RESISTANCE` for a HIGH zone and `LOW_CONFIDENCE_STRUCTURE` for MEDIUM or LOW; the short side mirrors with `SELLING_INTO_SUPPORT` (P03-S066 to P03-S069). INTERPRETATION: only the nearest zone is tested, with a one-sided pad (P03-S068).
- FACT: the breakout flags test the "broken" zone, the resistance with the greatest top at or below the entry (for a short, the support with the smallest bottom at or above it): `WEAK_BREAKOUT` when the last close clears the band by less than `weak_breakout_frac` of its width or when the last high poked above the band and the close did not; `NO_VOLUME_CONFIRMATION` on a breakout of either kind (a close beyond the band, or a poke through it that closed back) when the last volume is below `volume_surge_mult` times the average, and only when the average is above 0 (P03-S070, P03-S071). INTERPRETATION: the broken zone is chosen against the entry, at any distance and of any confidence; a breakout is counted whenever the last close is beyond the edge, however long the price has been there; the volume test compares a possibly partial last candle with full ones (P03-S072, P03-S184). FACT: the flags are de-duplicated in order (P03-S073); INTERPRETATION: each flag is appended at most once, so this changes nothing at the baseline (P03-S074).
- FACT: a V1 retest proposal exists only with `BUYING_INTO_RESISTANCE` or `SELLING_INTO_SUPPORT`: for a long, entry at the zone's top and stop `band_low × (1 − retest_sl_buffer_pct / 100)`; the short side mirrors (P03-S075, P03-S076). FACT and INTERPRETATION: the V1 proposal is computed after placement, parks nothing, changes nothing and is only written to the row; it is distinct from the V2 retest path (section 6) and from the PB-01 watchlist retest (A18's) (P03-S077). The `_pct` keys of the flags are percentages, `weak_breakout_frac` a fraction of the band width and `volume_surge_mult` a multiple (A02 K-21) (P03-S078, P03-S177). Edge inputs (a zero or NaN entry) are in P03-S079; the docstring's flag table is compared with the code in section 12 (P03-S080).

**The `sr_detector_results` row.**

- FACT: `_write` builds a dict of 24 keys in the order of the store's `_SR_INSERT_COLS` (the candidate's identity, `ts`, the two zones as compact JSON or `None`, the distances, the confidences or `"NONE"`, `confluence_evidence`, `breakout_volume`, the flags, `would_wait_for_retest`, the proposal's entry and stop, `structure_status`, `detector_version`, `created_at` and `actual_fill` as `None`); the evidence payload adds the anchors, the swings and `confidence_class` only when the anchors are on; the store call, then the telemetry counter, run inside one `try` whose failure is logged at ERROR "write failed" (P03-S081 to P03-S083, P03-S086). FACT (A03 Q-17): both A08 writers, the V1 observer and the V2 divert, pass all 24 keys in the same order (P03-S088, P03-S165).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (A03 H-11): `ts` is the candidate's stamp, set by A06's `_sr_observe` to `now_ist()` after the placement, and `created_at` the detector's clock at the write; both are aware IST values, written in the offset form; the naive form in the same table comes from the V2 divert (P03-S085, P03-S093, P03-S180). INTERPRETATION: a NaN or infinite value in a zone dict is written as the non-standard JSON tokens `NaN` or `Infinity` (P03-S084); if the detector's clock fails, `created_at` is NULL, the NOT NULL insert fails and the row is lost with an ERROR line (P03-S092).
- FACT: the table is "TABLE 38: sr_detector_results (SNR-DETECTOR-V1, schema v37)", "One row per PLACED candidate observed by the async, non-gating S&R detector. SHADOW analytics only" (`core/schema.sql` L1246 to L1300, A03's) (P03-S089). The V1 observer and the V2 divert write the 24 insert columns; the backfill writes `actual_fill`, `actual_result`, `win_loss` and `pnl`; no code writes `hypothetical_retest_result` (P03-S090). INTERPRETATION: a signal diverted by V2 and later placed through `continue_from_retest` would have a divert row (`snr-v2`) and an observer row (`snr-v1`) (P03-S094). FACT (A03 H-44): the V1 row's `mode` is the processor's mode lower-cased (`live` or `paper`) (P03-S095).
- FACT (readers, interface only): A15's `reports/daily_trade_review.py` keeps the first row per signal by `created_at` and displays the zone, distance and confidence columns; A12's `scripts/eod_cleanup.py` deletes the rows of pruned signals; the telemetry registry `config/expected_managers.yaml` lists the unit; of its four line references only `sr_detector/__init__.py:25` matches the baseline, while `main.py:2979`, `yaml:407` and `detector.py:254` match none of the baseline's lines (INTERPRETATION, P03-S099) (P03-S096 to P03-S099; P03-AP13). No A07 code reads the table (P03-S100).

**Is the observer non-gating? (the facts, with lines).**

- FACT: each of the three calls of `_sr_observe` (`signals/signal_processor.py` L1550 in `_admit_and_place`, L2296 in `continue_from_gate`, L2588 in `continue_from_retest`) follows the signal's `PROCESSED` write and the "placed" counters, so the observer sees a candidate only after its order was placed (P03-S101). FACT and INTERPRETATION: an ambiguous `place()` timeout marks the signal `TIMEOUT` and returns before the call, so a trade that the reconciler later adopts has no V1 row; an exception from the `PROCESSED` write, or inside the seam's own `Candidate` build, also leaves before the hand-off (P03-S215). The seam is A06's code and its own handler logs without a guard (P03-S102, P03-S110).
- FACT: the observer's only writes are the `sr_detector_results` row and the in-memory telemetry counter, besides its log lines; it calls no order, signal or trade method (P03-S104). INTERPRETATION: by its own code it takes no part in the placement it observes; the texts' "NEVER blocks/delays placement and NEVER raises into the pipeline" speak of the call and say nothing of the objects the worker shares with the order path: the live broker client and the process's rate limiter (up to three historical calls per candidate, four with the anchors), the database's single write lock (one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` insert per row), interpreter time, the instrument cache and the logging; the code neither bounds nor measures this use (P03-S105 to P03-S107, P03-S111, P03-S217). Whether any effect occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S112; P03-U08).
- FACT: at shutdown `sr_detector.stop()` runs after `signal_proc.stop()` (P03-S108). The committed switch is `sr_detector.enabled: true`, with the comment "★ SNR-V1 shadow ARMED — committed true 28-Jun", and `intraday_anchors_enabled: false` (values in the file only) (P03-S109).

**The end-of-day backfill (`scripts/sr_detector_backfill.py`).**

- FACT: the script locates the trading database from its own folder (`ROOT / "data_store" / "trading_system.db"`); `_is_terminal` accepts `CLOSED`, `CLOSED_MANUAL`, `CANCELLED`, `FAILED` and every status beginning with `REJECTED` (the schema trigger's terminal set); `_win_loss` maps the net P&L to `WIN`, `LOSS`, `FLAT` or `UNKNOWN` (P03-S113, P03-S114, P03-S120, P03-S174).
- FACT: `backfill(store, date_iso)` reads, through A03's `get_sr_results_for_backfill`, the rows of that `date(ts)` whose `actual_result` is NULL; for each it looks up the signal's newest `trades` row; with no trade it writes `NO_TRADE` / `NO_FILL` (comment "Observer fires post-placement, so by EOD a missing trade is terminal."); a non-terminal trade is skipped; a closed trade writes its exit reason (or status), win or loss and P&L; another terminal status writes the status, `NO_FILL` and the trade's P&L; each write is its own transaction (P03-S115, P03-S117 to P03-S119). INTERPRETATION: `date(ts)` is the UTC date of the offset stamp, which equals the IST date from 05:30 IST, a range that holds the committed session; a V2 divert row, stamped naive, is selected by its wall-clock date (P03-S116). Every write leaves `actual_result` non-NULL, so a re-run for the same date considers only the rows still NULL (A03 Q-29) (P03-S123, P03-S166).
- FACT: `main` takes an optional date (default today's IST date, falling back to the host's local date if the clock call fails) and `--db`, opens `StateStore(Path(args.db))` write-capable and prints one summary line; `_cron_main` runs A16's `skip_if_non_trading_day("sr_detector_backfill")` first and returns 0 on a non-trading day; the `__main__` block calls `_cron_main()` (P03-S124, P03-S127, P03-S132, P03-S136). INTERPRETATION: the date argument is not validated (P03-S125); a database error leaves `main` with a non-zero exit (P03-S126).
- INTERPRETATION (A03 D-09; entry A03-10): the script writes no heartbeat of its own; the only heartbeat is the guard's SKIPPED row on a non-trading day, written to the working-directory-relative trading database before `--db` is parsed (P03-S129, P03-S171, P03-S173).
- HISTORICAL and INTERPRETATION: `_cron_main`'s docstring says the guard is outside `main()` "so a manual/ad-hoc run on a non-trading day is never blocked"; the script's only command-line entry calls `_cron_main()`, so a documented manual run on a weekend or listed holiday, a re-run for an earlier date included, is skipped with exit status 0; only a call of `main()` from Python code bypasses the guard (P03-S130, P03-S131).
- FACT (declarations only): `config/cron_registry.yaml` L408 to L423 declares the job at 15:58 Monday to Friday, `market_day_only: true`, `enabled: true`; the generated cron line (`deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` L90 to L91, whose machine path is not reproduced) passes no date and records the exit status in a marker (P03-S133, P03-S134). INTERPRETATION (A03 U-25; A07 XS-12): the declared run handles only that day's rows, so a row skipped because its trade was still open at 15:58 is revisited only by a run given that date, which only a manual command-line run on a day the guard lets through, or a call of `main()` from code, makes; no A08 code schedules one (P03-S135, P03-S167, P03-S170).
- FACT (note N1): no A08 code creates, opens or writes a database other than the trading store (P03-S137, P03-S175). No non-test module imports or calls the backfill (P03-S138). Whether the script ever ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S141).

**Call sites in other topics' files (interface only).** The processor's constructor field and the three `_sr_observe` calls with what each passes (score `None` on the gate and retest paths), `main.py`'s switch, construction, injection and shutdown, the closure, the store methods and schema, the configuration keys, the clock, the telemetry, the heartbeat and holiday helpers, the cron declarations, the A15 report and the A12 prune are tabled in P03 section 9 (P03-S142 to P03-S162) and summarised in sections 9 and 11 below.

The intent sources are compared in section 12; the points for the AUDIT are P03-AP01 to P03-AP16 (section 15.1), the unknown items P03-U01 to P03-U10 (section 15.2) and the questions P03-R1 to P03-R5 (section 16).

## 6. The V2 retest path and the `continue_from_retest` handoff

This section covers the V2 retest mechanism ("SNR-V2"), which A08 owns and studies in full (D-41, P1): `sr_detector/retest_confirm.py` (`RetestParams`, `RetestResult`, `evaluate`, `_strong_close`) and `screening/retest_monitor.py` (`ParkedCandidate`, `RetestMonitor`, `RetestDiverter`), with A08's use of `retest_state`. **The D-41 P1 boundary, applied as decided:** for `SignalProcessor.continue_from_retest` (`signals/signal_processor.py` L2375 at the baseline, A06's file) A08 documents only the A08-specific handoff and values: what the monitor passes in, the retest-derived values that reach it (the zone band, the structure-stop buffer `retest_sl_buffer_pct` and the MARKET entry type of the retest entry) and what A08 receives back; its internal orchestration remains A06's and is not audited by A08, and the A09 and A10 decisions it reaches are recorded at the interface only. The PB-01 watchlist's retest confirmation stays A18's; A08 records only its reuse of A08 functions (D-41, P1; section 14).
