## 14. Cross-topic boundaries and handoffs

This section records, by owner topic, what crosses between A08 and each other topic at the baseline and every boundary point that the parts reported. The owners are those of the Master System Investigation Index; each other topic's code is read at the interface only (D-41, P3; P06-S005). A boundary point is reported here, not resolved: no record of another topic is edited, and no item is re-owned or re-classified (D-41, P4). The points named below are carried in full, with their source IDs, in section 15.1.

**The D-41 decisions as applied.** (P1) A08 owns the V2 retest mechanism and studies it in full (section 6); for `continue_from_retest` only the A08-specific handoff and values are recorded, its orchestration staying A06's and the A09 and A10 decisions it reaches being named at the interface only; the PB-01 watchlist's retest confirmation stays A18's, A08 recording only its reuse of A08 functions; A18 cites A08 for the retest path (P04-S093, P04-S017; P07-S101). (P2) A08 studies the V1 observer, its calculations, its `sr_detector_results` writes and the backfill (section 5); A18 later covers only the S&R shadow's activation controls, integration and Live activation status, citing A08 (P03-S003). (P3) Every call site of A08 code in other topics' files is recorded at the interface (sections 9 and 11). (P4) The 48 items and 16 entries are examined at A08's side only (section 13). (P5) The topic folder is `A08_TECHNICAL_ANALYSIS_SR_AND_RETEST_CONTEXT`.

**The retest-ownership wording (P07 section 6; note N6; P04-S150).** HISTORICAL and FACT: three wordings of the retest path's owner stand in approved records: A08 alone (A07 STUDY P02-S040; and, for the mechanism, D-41 P1); "A08 and A18" (A07 SL-06, SL-30 and the A07 AUDIT handoff rows L1208, L1214, L3436, with the section 11.4.1 rows L3761 and L3763); and "A18's retest path" (A06 PQ-13, A06 AUDIT L3990) (P07-S099 to P07-S103). D-41 P1 governs the topic boundary from 26-Nov-2026 and keeps A06's and A07's wordings unchanged; "A06's PQ-13 wording ("A18's retest path") is not changed; the A08 records note the difference" — this is that note. FACT: the path is wired under the S&R switch `sr_detector.wait_for_retest_enabled`; its two files import nothing from `v3_chain/`, `regime/` or any A18 file; A18's PB-01 retest is a distinct mechanism (P04-S150, P04-S016, P04-S017). INTERPRETATION: the wording difference needs no decision by Rama, D-41 having decided the boundary (P07-S104; P07 AP-06).

## 15. Points the AUDIT may examine, and what cannot be established

This section carries every Point for the AUDIT and every UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED item of the seven parts, so that none is dropped, softened or turned into a judgement. Each entry states what differs from what, with the facts and their sources in the parts' corrected wording (condensed, not changed in substance), and the comparison it invites; no point is resolved or classified here, KNOWN DEFECT is not used, and the classification is the AUDIT's (Document 04, section 4; Document 08). Where two or more parts raise the same point, it is listed once, under one number, with every source ID; nothing else is merged. "Boundary" marks a point whose code or record another topic owns; it is reported, not resolved. Points are numbered A08-AP-01 onward and unknown items A08-U-01 onward; the topic prefix keeps them apart from P07's own AP-01 to AP-06, and the hyphens keep the numbers clear of the repository's identifier check. The list follows the parts' order (P01 to P07); the STUDY section where the subject is described is given for each entry.

### 15.1 Points for the AUDIT

**Calculation core (P01; section 3).**

- **A08-AP-01** (P01-AP01; section 3) — What makes a zone HIGH: SR-C3, the configuration comment L526 and the first commit's message require "≥2 distinct method-types", and the module docstring says confidence is "NEVER a single method — it is a weighted sum of independent votes"; in the code `swing_pivots` and `multi_tf` are always present, both derived from the swing pivots, so with the committed weights the method-type condition is always met, every group present on all three timeframes is HIGH and a two-timeframe group with three touches is HIGH, with no volume, prior-day, round-number or recency vote; the test that defines HIGH treats `multi_tf` as one of the two methods (P01-S076 to P01-S080, P01-S121, P01-S124, P01-S125). Compare the code with the stated meaning of confidence. Boundary: the consumers that act on HIGH (the flags, the retest divert, A11's structure exit).
- **A08-AP-02** (P01-AP02; section 3) — Plateaus and outside bars: SR-P1's "a perfectly flat plateau does not mark every bar" and the docstring's "only in degenerate single-bar windows"; in the code every bar of a plateau with lower bars around it becomes a pivot and an outside bar yields both a HIGH and a LOW, so touches can count one swing several times (P01-S041 to P01-S043, P01-S056, P01-S122). Compare the touch count with SR-Z2's "touches = count of pivots".
- **A08-AP-03** (P01-AP03; section 3) — The width of a zone and of a merged group: clustering chains without a width limit (as SR-Z1 states), the merge compares with a drifting group mean and also joins zones of one timeframe, against SR-C1's "ACROSS timeframes"; the merged band is the union, gaps included (P01-S053, P01-S072, P01-S074, P01-S120). Compare an unbounded band with the design.
- **A08-AP-04** (P01-AP04; section 3) — Zero-width zones: a single pivot, or pivots at one price, make a zone with `band_low == band_high`, against the docstring "A price BAND (never a single line)"; such zones are scored and emitted, and the round-number test on them needs an exact multiple (P01-S054, P01-S079 (d), P01-S085 (d)).
- **A08-AP-05** (P01-AP05; sections 3, 10) — Candle order (A05 CM-05's A08 side): no step sorts or checks the order; `find_swing_pivots` uses list position as time, which leaves the zones unchanged for a descending series but reverses the output order that the regime engine and the V3 runners' `gate_htf` read as time, and for any other order takes positional neighbours that are not time neighbours; the fetch test's fixture is descending; `prior_day_levels` assumes ascending order (P01-S021, P01-S045, P01-S094, P01-S108, P01-S126, P01-S135). Boundary: A05 for the broker's rows, A18 for the regime and V3 runners, A07 for `gate_htf`.
- **A08-AP-06** (P01-AP06; section 3) — Duplicates and gaps: a duplicated bar can add a touch and doubles its volume in the profile; gaps are not detected; no source states how they were to be handled (P01-S046, P01-S095).
- **A08-AP-07** (P01-AP07; sections 3, 10) — Unvalidated values (A05 CM-10 and HA-08, A08's side): `from_kite` validates nothing and defaults a missing or `None` volume to 0; NaN silently suppresses pivots, while NaN or an infinity can raise `ValueError` or `OverflowError` in `volume_profile_nodes` and `_has_round_number`; mixed naive and aware times raise in two `max` calls; none of the four files catches an exception, and one bad row discards a whole interval in the fetcher (P01-S017 to P01-S019, P01-S047, P01-S058, P01-S064, P01-S086, P01-S097). Which caller handles each exception is the callers' logic.
- **A08-AP-08** (P01-AP08; section 3) — The recency time base: a `None` clock (a `_safe_now` that caught an exception, or a caller that passes none) or a non-positive window gives every dated zone the full recency vote rather than none; age is in calendar days; the time-zone branch strips both sides to wall-clock digits, which gives the elapsed time only when both wall clocks are IST, and the export's naive 15:30 `as_of` takes that branch whenever the labels are aware; `recency_min_factor` above 1 escapes the upper clamp (P01-S088 to P01-S091, P01-S137). Compare full credit on a missing clock with the intent.
- **A08-AP-09** (P01-AP09; sections 3, 8) — The buffer: the `ScoringParams` default `band_buffer_pct` is 0.0 while the zone builder, the loader and the committed file give 0.1, so the unit tests score without the buffer the configured path uses; SR-C2 does not mention the buffer, and the round-number test uses none (P01-S069, P01-S083, P01-S084, P01-S120, P01-S124).
- **A08-AP-10** (P01-AP10; section 3) — Zero-weight votes: the volume, prior-day and round-number votes are recorded and counted as method types even with a weight of 0 (latent at the committed weights) (P01-S081).
- **A08-AP-11** (P01-AP11; section 3) — The round-number rule: the step is half the power of ten below the band's centre, so the "magnitude" step adds nothing and the step jumps tenfold at each power of ten (from 5 to 50 per cent of price), against the docstring "A multiple of the price magnitude (or half-magnitude)"; a second, related rule lives in `round_number_levels` (P01-S084, P01-S085, P01-S087).
- **A08-AP-12** (P01-AP12; sections 3, 5) — Output order and the logged zones: `score_zones` returns zones by ascending centre, and the detector logs the first twelve of each kind in that order, so with more zones the lowest-priced are kept, not the strongest or nearest (P01-S070, P01-S093). Boundary: the detector's slicing (P03-S054).
- **A08-AP-13** (P01-AP13; sections 3, 4) — The volume profile's input: nodes come from the daily bars, or from the first series when there is no daily one (the PB-01 watchlist capture fetches only 60-minute and 30-minute bars), each bar's whole volume at its mid; `bins` and `node_frac` are not validated (P01-S062 to P01-S066, P01-S111). Boundary: the zone builder's choice (P02-S050).
- **A08-AP-14** (P01-AP14; sections 3, 7) — The confidence class: the comment L169 to L174 says swings are "NOT-YET-VALIDATED until Rama's manual-marking validation passes" and that "The 03.03 R:R gate will use ANCHOR-only levels for live capital until then"; no code sets the validated class, and nothing in the four files restricts the use of swing-derived zones, which the V3 runners use for their stop and target edges while labelling the record `ANCHOR_ONLY` (with gates commented "LOG-ONLY — recorded, never enforced"), and which the V2 divert and the structure exit would use when their flags are on (P01-S035, P01-S107, P01-S109, P01-S110, P01-S116, P01-S132). Boundary: A18 for the V3 consumers; the retest and structure-exit parts and A11 for theirs.
- **A08-AP-15** (P01-AP15; section 3) — Lag of recent structure: the last `right` bars can never be pivots (as SR-P2 states), so with the committed `n` of 5 the newest possible touch is five bars old on each timeframe (P01-S040, P01-S048).
- **A08-AP-16** (P01-AP16; sections 3, 12) — Documentation and residue points in the core: "Pure data — no behaviour"; "at _emit_signal_alert"; "spec E" and "spec K" with no specification tracked; "All CONFIG-tuned (visual-review phase)" while every committed value equals the code default; `PATHS.md` L417 "`sr_detector.enabled: false`" against the committed `true` (also in A08-AP-48); the unused `Zone.width`, `Zone.contains`, `Pivot.bar_index` and the unused import `field` in `confluence.py`; the MEDIUM test's comment "swing 4 + multi 1 = 5" leaves out the round-number vote that the code reading gives that band (5.5; boundary: A19's file) (P01-S009, P01-S012, P01-S013, P01-S022, P01-S025, P01-S032, P01-S122, P01-S129, P01-S140).
- **A08-AP-17** (P01-AP17; section 3) — Minor contract points: `ScoredZone.to_dict` omits `last_touch_ts` and rounds half to even on binary floats, while the bucket is decided on the unrounded score; `Candidate.is_long` treats every value other than LONG or BUY as short and raises on `None` (P01-S028, P01-S031, P01-S082).

**Fetch, zone builder, zone cache and warmer (P02; section 4).**

- **A08-AP-18** (P02-AP01; section 4) — The fetcher cache's key does not contain the window (A05 HA-06's A08 side; evidence that bears on A05's KNOWN DEFECT consideration, which stands as recorded): the key is `(symbol or cache key, interval)`; since `536172cfadfdd28ec9e5f8c00f9d549f0cd57dc2` a call can choose its own window under that key; SR-F3 states the key and was written when each fetcher asked one window per interval; `fetch_interval`'s docstring says its window is "a different window than the multi-day structural fetch" and that it "Reuses the same token/cache/fetch_fn machinery". Where a second window for one key can arise inside a non-zero TTL: no committed construction; the V1 fetcher with the anchors on and `anchor_intraday_interval: 30minute` (a value the loader accepts and the YAML comment lists), where a failed structural `30minute` fetch followed by a successful one-day anchor fetch would give later structural requests one day of bars as their 180-day series, with no log line; the export's per-call fetcher, whose clock is constant and whose `--interval` declares no `choices` and reaches the configuration copy through `model_copy`, which does not validate updated values; the PB-01 fetcher only at a zero or negative age. For the AUDIT to weigh on both sides: A05 HA-06 found its condition (d) to fail and its condition (c) to hold only under a documented but not committed setting (A05 AUDIT L1598); the further facts are that the key's fit to its requests changed with `536172cfadfdd28ec9e5f8c00f9d549f0cd57dc2`, that the docstring states the windows differ, and that the loader accepts `30minute` for both settings (P02-S028, P02-S040 to P02-S042, P02-S108, P02-S142, P02-S143, P02-S148, P02-S155, P02-S157, P02-S160).
- **A08-AP-19** (P02-AP02; sections 4, 10) — Wall-clock ages against the comment "monotonic": both caches age their entries by the injected wall clock (`now_ist`), while the field comment L49 names a monotonic time; a backward clock step serves entries beyond their TTL (and a TTL-0 entry at all), a forward step expires them early, and ages run through nights and holidays; A05 HA-06 records the fetcher's side; the zone cache's side is not in an earlier record (P02-S029 to P02-S031, P02-S069, P02-S144). Boundary: the machine's clock synchronisation is A17's.
- **A08-AP-20** (P02-AP03; section 4) — Two paths that raise despite "never raise", and one success reported as a failure: `_cache_get` and its clock read are outside the fetcher's `try`, and `fetch_by_token` converts the token outside it, against SR-F4's "nothing propagates into the pipeline", the module docstring and the methods' docstrings; a `_cache_put` failure after a successful broker call is logged as "historical_data failed" and the candles are discarded; argument types that no non-test caller passes would also raise outside the `try` (P02-S019, P02-S035, P02-S036, P02-S142, P02-S143). Whether either path occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **A08-AP-21** (P02-AP04; section 4) — One log text and level for every fetch failure, and silence for some outcomes (A05 HA-01 and IN-02; A05 HA-09 for the texts): "historical_data failed" at WARNING for a limiter timeout or a shutdown abort, in which no broker call was made, as for a broker error, the abort against `RateLimitAbortedError`'s own docstring "INFO (not WARN/ERROR)"; no log line for a token of 0, an empty answer or a missing handle; the docstrings' "the detector logs a fetch_failed row" and `main.py` L426's "the OhlcFetcher records a fetch_failed row" (P02-S020, P02-S027, P02-S037, P02-S038, P02-S107, P02-S109, P02-S110). Boundary: the limiter and its exceptions are A04's; the detector's row is P03's; the `main.py` text is A01's and A05's.
- **A08-AP-22** (P02-AP05; sections 4, 10) — Nothing is ever evicted: the fetcher's `_mem` and the zone cache's `_store` are never pruned or cleared; `evict_expired`, `size` and `clear` exist with no caller; nothing clears them at the end of the day or at shutdown; the memory held grows with the distinct symbols for the life of the process; the texts speak of "intra-session" re-fetch suppression and a cache "within the session", and no source states an entry's intended lifetime (P02-S032, P02-S064, P02-S068, P02-S073, P02-S143, P02-S149). Boundary: whether the process restarted daily is A16's and A17's (P02-U10).
- **A08-AP-23** (P02-AP06; section 4) — The re-warm rule re-warms every stored symbol without end: the scan returns expired entries too, so each symbol warmed once is re-warmed about every `ttl − margin` seconds for the life of the process, after the close and on holidays included, and a symbol whose re-warm gets no interval at all is queued again at every idle scan (about every 2 s plus the failed fetch), each attempt asking the closure, and so the shared `historical` bucket, for every interval, except after a failed token lookup or without a market-data handle; the docstrings say "Re-warms cached symbols before TTL expiry", "whose age exceeds (ttl − margin)" and "re-warm a cached symbol this long before its TTL expiry" (P02-S067, P02-S084, P02-S145, P02-S146, P02-S149). Boundary: the bucket and its other users are A04's and A18's; the load at runtime is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **A08-AP-24** (P02-AP07; section 4) — A `ZoneSet`'s age is not its candles' age: the zone cache ages from the `put`, while the warmer's fetcher serves candles for up to its own TTL, so at the committed values a set reported as at most about 1500 s old can rest on candles about 3000 s old, and about 3600 s at the extreme; the set records neither the candles' age nor the intervals used (P02-S063, P02-S065, P02-S070). Boundary: the readers are the diverter and A11's structure exit; no source states a maximum age for the data behind a divert or a structure stop.
- **A08-AP-25** (P02-AP08; section 4) — Partial and empty results stored like complete ones, and two treatments of a missing daily series: `warm` stores whatever non-empty subset of the intervals came back, and an empty zone set, for which it returns `True` while its docstring says "Returns True if zones were cached"; `scored_zones_from_candles` takes the prior-day levels and volume nodes from the first intraday series when the daily one is missing, while `build_anchor_payload` gives an empty `prior_day` in the same case; nothing records which series was used (P02-S050, P02-S060, P02-S086). Boundary: the effect on HIGH zones is P01-S079 (c); the readers' use is P04's and A11's.
- **A08-AP-26** (P02-AP09; section 4) — The prior day is chosen by list position: `prior_day_levels` uses `daily[-2]` whatever the dates, which is the previous trading day only when the series ends with a candle for today, and otherwise the day before; a one-candle series gives that candle, and a descending or duplicated series other candles; whether the broker included today's candle is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the test states the today-candle case only; no source states which day was meant (P02-S048, P02-S152).
- **A08-AP-27** (P02-AP10; sections 4, 8) — The warmer's queue size, idle sleep and enable flag are fixed in the code (A02 K-23's A08 side): `main.py` passes none of them, no configuration key reaches them, and the configurable `sr_detector.max_queue` governs only the V1 detector's queue (P02-S075, P02-S076, P02-S102). Boundary: the keys and their absence are A02's.
- **A08-AP-28** (P02-AP11; P04-AP14; P06-AP02; sections 4, 6, 9) — Partial state after a failure in an optional S&R block, and the warmer's lifecycle edges (A01 C37's A08 side; C37 unchanged): the block comments say a failure means "continuing without it" or "no retest", and the V2 retest block's comment says "both daemons stop in _shutdown" (L3767 to L3768); by the code, (a) the V2 retest block binds the diverter and the square-off's reference before the starts, and its `except` resets only `retest_monitor`, so a failed thread start can leave diverts parking candidates in a monitor that never polls, with `_shutdown` unaware of it [P04-AP14; P06-AP02 (a)]; (b) a V2 retest failure before L3796 with the structure exit off leaves a warmer that the processor holds but that is never started, whose queue fills and then drops each new symbol with a WARNING [P02-AP11; P06-AP02 (b)]; (c) the structure-exit block leaves the warmer started when the manager fails, and the V2 infrastructure block counts its fetcher before building the warmer [P06-AP02 (c), (d)]; (d) the warmer's `stop` joins for 2.0 s without checking the outcome, lets a running warm finish later (a limiter wait has by then been aborted by the shutdown event, while a broker call is not interrupted), does not drain the queue and leaves `enqueue` accepting symbols; a later `start` could run a second worker, and `start` is not locked [P02-AP11] (P02-S077 to P02-S078, P02-S088, P02-S089; P04-S091, P04-S152; P06-S079, P06-S138, P06-S181). Boundary: the wiring and the shutdown order are A01's.
- **A08-AP-29** (P02-AP12; section 4) — The dedup covers only queued symbols, and each signal triggers a warm: a symbol can be queued again while it is warmed; a fresh `ZoneSet` does not prevent a new warm on each new signal; a symbol accepted while no worker runs is never removed from `_pending`; no source states whether a signal for a symbol with fresh zones was meant to trigger a warm (P02-S081).
- **A08-AP-30** (P02-AP13; sections 4, 10) — Concurrency inside the fetcher: no single-flight (two threads that miss on one key both fetch), no serialization of closure calls on one object or across objects, and a cache hit returns the shared, mutable list object (P02-S033, P02-S034). Boundary: whether the one client tolerated overlapping calls is A01 C38a's and A04 RL-11's UNKNOWN; the A18 consumers' handling of a shared list is theirs.
- **A08-AP-31** (P02-AP14; P06-AP06; sections 4, 8) — Windows, TTLs and tuning keys set per construction and shared across sections (A02 K-24's A08 side): `fetch_timeframes` always uses the constructor's lookback, so the PB-01 capture's daily fetch takes the V3 section's 180 days and not `capture_fetch_lookback_days`; the regime fetcher's cache TTL is `sr_detector.cache_ttl_sec` while its cadence is the regime section's; the PB-01 fetcher asks two windows each for `day` and `30minute`, kept apart by its TTL of 0, which the `main.py` comment gives as the means to keep the entry stage's 5-minute poll fresh; the one-minute fetcher's "no cache" is a TTL of 0 under which every list is still stored [P02-AP14]; the V3 chain and two of the three PB-01 consumers receive knobs and scoring built from the `sr_detector` section (comment "REUSE the sr_detector zone knobs (no duplicate tuning)"), while the block's comments name only the S&R detector and the V2 path, so a change of one of these keys also changes the A18 consumers without the block saying so [P06-AP06] (P02-S031, P02-S040, P02-S042, P02-S103, P02-S148; P06-S063, P06-S179). Boundary: the constructions are A18's and A01's file; the keys are A02's.
- **A08-AP-32** (P02-AP15; section 4) — The anchors and the round numbers (off at the committed values): `round_number_levels` raises for a NaN or infinite reference price against its docstring's "returns ()", and the detector's call of `build_anchor_payload` is outside the detector's `try`; the VWAP and the ORB have no completeness rule, so a call during the opening window or with a forming bar uses partial data; the scoping fits labels that mark the start of a bar in IST wall-clock time; the anchor payload is unrounded while the swing payload rounds to four decimals; the swing payload lists the merged group's values under each contributing role (P02-S054, P02-S056 to P02-S058, P02-S060, P02-S062). Boundary: the detector's handling is P03's; `session_vwap` is A05's (CM-15).
- **A08-AP-33** (P02-AP16; sections 4, 12) — Documentation and residue points in the fetch layer: `zone_cache.py`'s "Phase A reads resistance ... Phase B will read support" while the divert reads both sides; `PATHS.md` L386 and `main.py` L3542 to L3544 describe the cache and the warmer as dormant whenever the retest switch is off, while the structure-exit switch alone also builds them; the fetch docstring's "fetch the 3 timeframe windows" and "cache within the session"; the YAML's "intra-session"; the unused `field` import in `zone_cache.py`; the expected-manager registry gives the retest switch as the only reason for the cache's and the warmer's absence (P02-S008, P02-S143, P02-S145, P02-S148, P02-S149, P02-S154, P02-S158). Boundary: `main.py` is A01's; `PATHS.md` is a legacy document; the registry is A15's.
- **A08-AP-34** (P02-AP17; P06-AP10; sections 4, 9, 11) — The zone cache is filled only from signal arrivals, and the structure exit alone: the warmer is fed only by the dispatcher's `_warm_zones` and its own re-warm scan, and neither reader enqueues, so a symbol has a `ZoneSet` only after a signal for it has reached the dispatcher in the current process; a position whose symbol has had no signal since the process started gets `None`, and the structure exit then leaves its stop where it is [P02-AP17]; the code allows the structure exit with the retest path off (switch row 4), and the comment L3814 to L3815 names that case [P06-AP10]; no comment or document describes how zones reach a position without a signal in the current process (P02-S072, P02-S159; P06-S088, P06-S091). Boundary: the structure exit's handling is A11's; the dispatcher is A06's.

**V1 observer, flags, row and backfill (P03; section 5).**

- **A08-AP-35** (P03-AP01; sections 5, 10) — Indirect effects of the observer on the order path: the texts say the observer "NEVER blocks/delays placement and NEVER raises into the pipeline"; by its own code it is reached only after the placement and the `PROCESSED` write, hands off without waiting, raises nothing for a `Candidate` and writes nothing that the order path reads; its worker shares with the order path the live broker client and the process's rate limiter, the database's single write lock (one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` insert with a `synchronous = FULL` commit per row), interpreter time, the instrument cache and the logging, and no text states an intent or a bound for these (P03-S101 to P03-S107, P03-S111, P03-S193, P03-S217). Boundary: A01 and A04 for the client and the limiter (A01 C38a, B-11; A04 RL-05, RL-11), A03 for the store's locking, A05 and A15 for the instrument cache and the logging.
- **A08-AP-36** (P03-AP02; section 5) — As of when the V1 evidence is computed (A05 HA-04's A08 side): the stated question is whether buying into resistance can be detected "before the outcome is known", but each analysis fetches windows that end when the worker reaches the candidate, after any queue wait and with cached lists up to 1800 s old, and nothing truncates the candles to the placement-time stamp or drops a still-forming last candle; a row can therefore rest partly on price action after the entry (P03-S043, P03-S053, P03-S057, P03-S168, P03-S194). Whether the broker returned forming candles is UNKNOWN (A08-U-02).
- **A08-AP-37** (P03-AP03; section 5) — What the breakout flags measure: the "breakout candle" is the last element of the 30-minute list at processing time, not a candle tied to the entry; the "broken" zone is the nearest lower resistance (or upper support) at any distance and of any confidence; a breakout is counted whenever the last close is beyond that zone's edge, however long the price has been there and without any candle having crossed it, and also when the last candle's high (for a short, its low) passed the edge while its close did not; `NO_VOLUME_CONFIRMATION` is impossible when the average volume is 0, is always raised on a breakout when the list holds a single candle with a positive volume, and compares a possibly partial last candle's volume with full candles (P03-S052, P03-S053, P03-S070 to P03-S072, P03-S184). Compare with the docstring's "breakout candle lacks follow-through" and "breakout without a volume surge" (P03-S014).
- **A08-AP-38** (P03-AP04; sections 5, 12) — The flag table and the committed comment against the code: `BUYING_INTO_RESISTANCE` and its mirror test only the nearest zone with a one-sided pad; `LOW_CONFIDENCE_STRUCTURE` requires the entry to be "into" the nearest zone; `WEAK_BREAKOUT` includes a poke-and-fail; `NO_CLEAR_STRUCTURE` is raised only when no zone exists at all, a LOW zone counting as structure, and is also the only flag of a `FETCH_FAILED` row; the comment "entry within this % of a HIGH zone" (`config/system_config.yaml` L517) describes a pad the code applies to the nearest zone of any confidence; `PATHS.md` L418 omits `SELLING_INTO_SUPPORT` (P03-S014, P03-S044, P03-S048, P03-S066 to P03-S069, P03-S080, P03-S196, P03-S203).
- **A08-AP-39** (P03-AP05; sections 5, 10) — Placed trades that leave no row: the schema says "One row per PLACED candidate observed by the async, non-gating S&R detector"; a placement leaves no row when its `place()` call timed out ambiguously (the signal is marked `TIMEOUT` and `_sr_observe` is never called, even if the recovery later adopts the trade) or when the `PROCESSED` write or the seam's own `Candidate` build raises; and a candidate handed to the observer leaves no row when the queue is full (one WARNING), when it is still queued at `stop` or at any other end of the process, or is cut off by the interpreter's exit (no log line), when the analysis raises (one ERROR) or when the insert fails, a NULL `created_at` after a clock failure included (one ERROR); nothing in the table records these gaps (P03-S033, P03-S038, P03-S055, P03-S086, P03-S092, P03-S215, P03-S216). Boundary: A06 (and A10 for the recovery) for the cases before the hand-off.
- **A08-AP-40** (P03-AP06; section 5) — "A later run picks it up" (A03 U-25's A08 side; A07 XS-12): every run selects only its date's rows and the declared 15:58 run passes no date, so a row skipped as open is revisited only by a run given that date; the finding is A03's U-25, and this point adds the guard's effect on manual runs (A08-AP-41) (P03-S115, P03-S124, P03-S135, P03-S167).
- **A08-AP-41** (P03-AP07; section 5) — The guard and manual runs: `_cron_main`'s docstring says the guard is outside `main()` "so a manual/ad-hoc run on a non-trading day is never blocked", and the message of `09ea7254cc47b5c211fb6d408d92d3e62d859b73` says the guard is "never in main(), so manual/ad-hoc runs on a weekend (e.g. re-issuing a token) are never blocked" and that it "sits behind each script's existing `__main__`"; the script's only command-line entry calls `_cron_main()`, so a documented manual run on a weekend, or on an NSE holiday that the calendar lists, a re-run for an earlier date included, is skipped with exit status 0 and can write a SKIPPED heartbeat; only a call of `main()` from Python code bypasses it (P03-S127, P03-S130, P03-S131, P03-S213). Boundary: A16 for the guard and for the same entry pattern in the other scripts of that commit.
- **A08-AP-42** (P03-AP08; sections 5, 10) — Dates and time zones in the backfill: rows are selected by SQLite's `date(ts)`, the UTC date of the offset IST stamps (the IST date only from 05:30 IST); the default date falls back to the host's local date when `now_ist` fails; the guard's "today" is the IST date; the schedule runs in the host's cron time zone, which the cron file's comment says is IST but does not set (P03-S116, P03-S125, P03-S128, P03-S134, P03-S207). Boundary: A03 (H-11, the `DATE()` reading), A16 (the cron time zone).
- **A08-AP-43** (P03-AP09; sections 5, 6) — The backfill and the V2 divert's rows (latent at the committed values): the selection does not filter on `detector_version`, so `snr-v2` divert rows are backfilled too; a divert row whose signal was never placed is closed as `NO_TRADE` on the comment's premise "Observer fires post-placement", which a pre-placement row does not meet; a signal placed through the retest path can have a divert row and an observer row, and the A15 report keeps the one with the earlier `created_at` text, the two rows having different time forms (P03-S093, P03-S094, P03-S096, P03-S115, P03-S122). Boundary: P04 (the divert row), A15 (the report), A03 (H-11).
- **A08-AP-44** (P03-AP10; P04-AP19; P06-AP05; sections 5, 6, 8) — Stop-buffer keys for one formula (A02 K-22's A08 side; primary part P04; K-22 unchanged): the V1 proposal's stop uses `retest_sl_buffer_pct`, a loader field absent from the committed file (0.3), while the V2 stop uses the committed `sl_buffer_pct` (0.2), with the same `band_low × (1 − pct/100)` formula [P03-AP10]; the latter reaches the processor through a parameter named `retest_sl_buffer_pct`, and within the V2 path the divert row and the resumption agree [P04-AP19]; a third key, `structure_exit.sl_buffer_pct` (0.2), feeds the structure exit, its comment saying "match Phase A", with nothing in the code tying the two 0.2 values [P06-AP05] (P03-S060, P03-S075, P03-S077, P03-S178; P04-S097, P04-S131; P06-S026, P06-S051, P06-S066).
- **A08-AP-45** (P03-AP11; section 5) — The outcome values written by the backfill: `NO_FILL` is written for cancelled, failed and rejected trades together with whatever fill price and P&L the trade row holds; a closed trade without P&L gets `UNKNOWN`; `actual_result` falls back to the status when `exit_reason` is empty; none of these value sets is stated in a column comment or a document read (P03-S118, P03-S121). Boundary: A10 and A03 for what such a trade row can hold.
- **A08-AP-46** (P03-AP12; section 5) — The store's comment on the insert columns (A03's file; boundary): "actual_*/win_loss/pnl/hypothetical_retest_result are EOD-backfilled and intentionally absent here" (`core/state_store.py` L1967 to L1969), while `actual_fill` is in `_SR_INSERT_COLS` and both A08 writers pass it as `None` (P03-S081, P03-S088, P03-S196).
- **A08-AP-47** (P03-AP13; P04-AP16; sections 5, 6, 12) — The effect-telemetry registry's line references (A02's file, A15's mechanism; boundary): `config/expected_managers.yaml` L118 to L123 cite `main.py:2979`, `yaml:407` and `detector.py:254 _write` for the V1 detector, which match none of the baseline's lines (`main.py` L3494, `config/system_config.yaml` L509, `sr_detector/detector.py` L259 and L298) [P03-AP13]; L351 to L366 cite "yaml:427", "main.py:3041", "main.py:3047", "main.py:3263", "main.py:3268" and "main.py:3253" for the V2 objects, which match none of the baseline lines (switch L529; constructions L3557, L3564, L3784, L3789; import L3773) [P04-AP16] (P03-S098, P03-S099; P04-S125).
- **A08-AP-48** (P03-AP14; P06-AP03; P06-AP04; sections 5, 9, 12) — The legacy and wiring texts' account of the V1 observer: the wiring comment "Both default OFF → nothing constructed" (L3418 to L3419), the loader docstring "When disabled (the default)" (L1159) and `PATHS.md` L417 ("`sr_detector.enabled: false`") describe the switch as off, while the file has `enabled: true` since `f2098656d7fb0d9a4cf917c915eee2dd909b1e41` (2026-06-28) and `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L234 says "`enabled` stays true"; A01 S-35 records the wiring comment as MATCH with this note (unchanged) [P06-AP03; P03-AP14]; `PATHS.md` and `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` describe the seam as two call sites ("BOTH `_process_one` + `continue_from_gate`"; "both paths"), the A08 inventory's DRAFT section 2.1 listed one (L1550), and the code has three (L1550, L2296, L2588) [P03-AP14; P06-AP04]; the arming commit calls the observer "read-only", while it writes its rows [P03-AP14] (P03-S101, P03-S203, P03-S204, P03-S214; P06-S065, P06-S093, P06-S122, P06-S179 to P06-S184; P01-S032, P01-S129). The inventory's listing was corrected in its Revision 1.
- **A08-AP-49** (P03-AP15; section 5) — The nearest zones and distances in the row: the recorded "nearest resistance" can lie below the entry (and the "nearest support" above it) when no zone is on the expected side, its distance is then negative, distances are measured to the zone's centre, and the column comments state no unit or sign; the A15 report displays these columns (P03-S064, P03-S065, P03-S091, P03-S096).
- **A08-AP-50** (P03-AP16; section 5) — Minor contract points of the V1 observer: the JSON columns can hold the non-standard tokens `NaN` and `Infinity` and are spaced differently by the two writers; `stop` reports neither a timed-out join nor the candidates it leaves in the queue, and a `start` after a timed-out `stop` would run a second worker (no caller does so); `_cfg` is stored and never read, and the detector's `_mode` serves only a log line (P03-S024, P03-S033, P03-S084).

**V2 retest path and the resumption handoff (P04; section 6).**

- **A08-AP-51** (P04-AP01; section 6) — The monitor's elapsed time at the committed wiring: `_evaluate` subtracts the naive divert time (`added_at`, made naive at L368 and restored naive by `_from_row`; field comment L40 "naive IST") from the monitor's `now_fn()` (L171), and `main.py` gives the monitor the aware `time_authority.now_ist` (L3787; `core/time_authority.py` L94 to L99); by the Python documentation that subtraction raises `TypeError`, so every evaluation fails after its fetch and, with the switch on, no candidate would be confirmed, rejected or timed out, against the docstrings' "CONFIRMED → hand to signal_processor.continue_from_retest" and "REJECT (timeout / break-down) → clear" (L9 to L10) and the timeout of `retest_confirm` (L28); the tests give the monitor a naive clock and so do not show it; whether the switch was ever on is UNKNOWN (P04-S005, P04-S006, P04-S070, P04-S168, P04-S172). P04-X08 (d): it bears on SL-06, SL-30, ET-07, EV-11, Q-11, A01 C13 and the reachability of `continue_from_retest`; its classification is for the AUDIT under the A01 four-condition rule.
- **A08-AP-52** (P04-AP02; section 6) — The end-of-day clear writes no status: `clear_all` deletes the rows and empties memory, so the cleared signals keep `RETEST_WAITING` with no final status, while the schema comment says the row is deleted "atomically on release (confirm / reject / EOD)" and the store's release writes a status with the delete; such signals are outside the prune predicate; a poll whose snapshot predates the clear can still release a cleared candidate (writing `RETEST_CONFIRMED` or `RETEST_REJECTED_*` after the clear) and hand it to the resumption (P04-S059, P04-S085, P04-S087, P04-S165).
- **A08-AP-53** (P04-AP03; section 6) — The duplicate guard: the comment L344 to L346 says "a symbol already parked must not spawn a 2nd MARKET entry (overlap invariant). Drop the duplicate signal rather than placing it into the zone", but the test runs only after a zone match, so a later signal of a parked symbol that matches no zone goes to normal placement while the parked candidate can still resume; the symbol is free again as soon as a confirmation's release has run, before the resumption; the test and `register` are separate critical sections whose serialization the comment L329 to L331 attributes to A06's upstream claim (A06's to confirm) (P04-S041, P04-S108 (d), P04-S167, P04-S181).
- **A08-AP-54** (P04-AP04; section 6) — Release before resumption (A03 Q-11's A08 side; Q-11 unchanged): the comment L181 to L182 says the row is released first "so it can't be re-evaluated"; when the release's store call fails the memory entry is gone, the row and old status stay, the callback runs, and a restart restores the row, which can then be confirmed and resumed again; meanwhile the per-symbol guard is lost and a second candidate of the same symbol can be parked, so two rows of one symbol can be restored (P04-S072, P04-S082, P04-S083, P04-S135).
- **A08-AP-55** (P04-AP05; section 6) — The strong-close wording against the code: the docstrings and the committed and loader comments say the close must be "in the UPPER frac" or "in the strong 60%" of the range, which read literally admits a close at 40 % of the range or above; the code and the `RetestParams` field comment require a position of at least the fraction (60 %, the top 40 % of the range) (P04-S029, P04-S030, P04-S163).
- **A08-AP-56** (P04-AP06; sections 6, 12) — Long-only texts after the short mirror: the monitor's docstring, two schema comments (A03's), two processor comments (A06's) and a loader comment (A02's) describe a long-only design that the code no longer has since `02357130fe09fab38833e81ce59f1b0f407488bc` (P04-S164, P04-S180).
- **A08-AP-57** (P04-AP07; section 6) — Stored or declared and never used: `retest_state.sizing_inputs` ("JSON snapshot of inputs to re-size at confirm") is restored but not read by the resumption, which re-derives its inputs; `retest_state.timeout_at` ("deadline") is written and never read, the timeout in force being the monitor's current `timeout_sec`, so a changed `retest_timeout_sec` would apply to restored candidates whatever their stored deadline; `IN_PROGRESS_STATES`, `RetestResult.terminal` and `parked_count` have no non-test reader; `field` is imported and unused and `json` is imported twice (P04-S012, P04-S013, P04-S020, P04-S052, P04-S075, P04-S166).
- **A08-AP-58** (P04-AP08; section 6) — The stored state is not monotonic: after a fetch with no candles the snapshot is written back to `WAIT_BREAKOUT` and later advanced again, against the docstring's "re-seeing candles never mis-advances"; the outcome is not affected, the state not being an input (P04-S076, P04-S162).
- **A08-AP-59** (P04-AP09; sections 6, 10) — Rehydration has no date and no status filter: every row is restored whatever its day or its signal's status, while the end-of-day comment intends that none "survive into the next session" (`orders/eod_squareoff.py` L349 to L350) and A18's analogous PB-01 stage, which calls itself "the direct analog" of `RetestMonitor`, states an anti-rehydration rule that loads "ONLY today's rows" (`v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L6 to L7, L17 to L19) (P04-S017, P04-S081, P04-S084).
- **A08-AP-60** (P04-AP10; sections 6, 10) — The minute candles used: the monitor applies no as-of truncation or completeness test, so a still-forming minute (if the broker sent one) enters the fold and can cause an immediate, irreversible confirmation or rejection; its filter by the divert time excludes the divert's own minute under the start-of-minute reading of the label and includes it under the end reading; no source states which was intended (A05 HA-03, HA-04; history questions A05-17 and A05-29 OPEN); for comparison, A18's PB-01 stage states "only CLOSED 5-min candles are ever evaluated (`truncate_to_asof` drops a still-forming bar)" (P04-S066 to P04-S068, P04-S142).
- **A08-AP-61** (P04-AP11; section 6) — The direction is tested in different forms along the path: case-insensitive and stripped in `evaluate` (`_is_long`), exact in the audit row (L399) and in the resumption (`signals/signal_processor.py` L2435), and through the side in the diverter (L321 to L322); they agree for the two values the strategy schema admits and would disagree for any other stored value (P04-S018, P04-S105, P04-S111).
- **A08-AP-62** (P04-AP12; sections 6, 11) — The monitor's rejection statuses are outside the `REJECTED*` family: `RETEST_REJECTED_TIMEOUT`, `_BREAK_DOWN` and `_RECLAIM_UP` begin with `RETEST_`, so the dashboard funnel counts them as "accepted", the signal-status report as "other", and the end-of-day prune never takes them, while the divert's own `REJECTED_RETEST_DUP` and the resumption's `REJECTED_RETEST_BAD_STRUCTURE` are "rejected" and prunable (the readers' logic is their owners') (P04-S057, P04-S059, P04-S060, P04-S103).
- **A08-AP-63** (P04-AP13; P06-AP01; sections 6, 9) — The boot order of the A08 threads (A01 C13's A08 side; C13 unchanged): the sources cited by C13 put every thread start after the startup reconcile and its kill check (A01 AUDIT L851: MAIN8, MAIN10, MAIN11) [P06-AP01]; the A08 threads start during construction (L3504; L3796, L3797, L3816 with the V2 or structure-exit switch), before `eod.post_wire_init()`, the reconcile, the kill check and the processor's start; when the kill check returns 1 (L3899, in `_main_locked`) or an exception leaves `_main_locked`, `_shutdown` is not called and the A08 daemons end with the interpreter [P06-AP01]; the monitor's thread starts at L3797, before the startup reconcile that, by its comment, "MUST precede" any sizing or placement (L3869 to L3879), so by the code's structure a rehydrated candidate could be resumed on the monitor's thread from its first poll, 20 s after start; at the committed wiring the evaluation raises before any callback (A08-AP-51) [P04-AP13] (P04-S092, P04-S151; P06-S081 to P06-S083, P06-S137).
- **A08-AP-64** (P04-AP15; section 6) — Bookkeeping failures that the caller does not see: a failed `retest_state` insert leaves a memory-only parking that the diverter cannot detect and a restart loses (`register` "Never raises"); a failed `RETEST_WAITING` write leaves a parked candidate whose signal shows `PASSED` and has no divert row; `clear_all` reports the in-memory count, not the rows deleted; `stop` resets `_worker` even when its join timed out; conditional on A06's derivation and A03's storage, a NaN pre-divert stop with a finite entry would fail the `NOT NULL` insert and give a memory-only parking (P04-S044, P04-S074, P04-S085, P04-S089, P04-S137).
- **A08-AP-65** (P04-AP17; section 6) — For A03 at the boundary: the legacy security audit's statement that "two concurrent diverts could create two rows" (`docs/audit/system_security_audit_02jul2026.md` L154) against A03's MATCH reading of the sub-select upsert (Q-46) and the store's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transactions; and the table has no per-symbol limit (P04-S058, P04-S178).
- **A08-AP-66** (P04-AP18; sections 6, 11) — For A10 at the boundary: the retest entry's MARKET type is honoured by the limit-triple protocol (price 0.0) and, by its own comment, ignored by the CO protocol ("CO entry is always order_type="SL""), so the docstring's "places a MARKET entry" (`signals/signal_processor.py` L2380) holds only for the protocol chosen; which protocol applied is A10's (P04-S100).
- **A08-AP-67** (P04-AP20; section 12) — For A19 at the boundary: the V2 tests state their expectations with naive clocks and fakes; no test runs the monitor with the wiring's aware clock, starts or stops its thread, fails a release, restores two rows of one symbol, or covers a flat bar, a duplicated or unordered candle or a NaN value in the fold (P04-S171 to P04-S175).
- **A08-AP-68** (P04-AP21; section 14) — Records notes (no record is changed): A03 H-11 cites `screening/retest_monitor.py` "L229, L239", `timeout_at` "L230" and `ts` "L398 and L415" (baseline: L230, L231, L239, L414 from L398); A03 P-11 cites "L370 to L371" for memory-first persistence (baseline: `register` L95 to L97); A03 Q-11 cites `rehydrate` "L117 to L132" (baseline: L118 to L136); the part brief cites `orders/order_protocol_limit.py` L207 (a comment; the decision lines are L210 and L216); `PATHS.md` L387 cites the divert at ":818" (baseline L1042); the A08 inventory's DRAFT list of 26 test files omitted `tests/unit/test_sr_v2_market_path.py` (corrected in its Revision 1) (P04-S100, P04-S129, P04-S132, P04-S134, P04-S135, P04-S176).

**Scripts (P05; section 7).**

- **A08-AP-69** (P05-AP01; section 7) — The export cannot produce its output at the baseline: by reading, its real wiring stops at an import (whether one fails depends on the environment) or else at L139, with a `TypeError` at `core/config_loader.py` L2481, and has done so at every commit of its history; as written, the steps after L139 would also fail (L141, L142), and L143 would give it no client and so no data; its docstring, `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L233 and `PATHS.md` L209 describe a working export, and the tests cover only the pure core (P05-S047 to P05-S061, P05-S088, P05-S115, P05-S118). Boundary: `load_all`, `InstrumentCache` and `_build_market_data_kite` are A02's, A05's and A01's; the call sites are A08's.
- **A08-AP-70** (P05-AP02; P07 AP-02; sections 7, 14) — The export's recorded failing line: A01 C38b records L141 (`AttributeError`, raised before L143; A01 AUDIT L2062 and the section 8 row L2186, "the script fails at its own L141"); A02 records L139 ("INTERPRETATION: `TypeError`"; A02 AUDIT L529 and the handoff row L554); A05 records that the script "stops at L139" before C38b's L141, "(if its imports succeed)"; A04 records that it "fails before `_build_market_data_kite`", which fits either line [P07 AP-02]; by reading, L139 fails first whenever the imports complete, whether they complete is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and L141's `AttributeError` is what that line does taken alone; C38b says "its owner classifies the failure" [P05-AP02] (P05-S059 to P05-S061, P05-S093; P07-S022, P07-S089, P07-S095). No earlier record is changed.
- **A08-AP-71** (P05-AP03; sections 7, 12) — The condition the export was to meet: the committed comments make the manual-marking validation, made from the export's CSV, the condition for turning `intraday_anchors_enabled` on and for flipping `confidence_class`; the legacy pending lists record the validation as pending until at least 01-Aug-2026; by reading, the export could not produce the CSV (P05-S089 to P05-S091, P05-S116). Whether the validation was made another way is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (A08-U-33).
- **A08-AP-72** (P05-AP04; section 7) — The market-data handle: the export's docstring and comments say it reuses `main.py`'s "read-only market-data kite", and the helper's docstring says "Live: reuse kite_client (== adapter._kite)"; the export calls the helper with `is_paper=False, kite_client=None` and so would receive `None`, after which its closure returns `[]` with no broker call (P05-S056 to P05-S058, P05-S115). Boundary: the helper is A01's; A05 HA-09's point (3), "never None in Live", holds for the trading process, not for this call (P05-S108).
- **A08-AP-73** (P05-AP05; section 7) — L141 and L142 against the instrument cache's documented use: `InstrumentCache(str(path))` and `instrument_cache.load()` against `InstrumentCache.load(Path(...))` and "Called by load(); not typically called directly" (`core/instrument_cache.py` L79 to L80, L90) (P05-S054, P05-S055). Boundary: the class is A05's.
- **A08-AP-74** (P05-AP06; section 7) — A fetch failure leaves no trace in the export's output: the docstring says a `None` analysis (fetch failure) contributes no rows; the real `analyze` returns a fetch-failed analysis instead; either way no row and no marker is written, the printed count counts rows only, and a pair whose daily request alone failed cannot be told apart from a pair without those levels (P05-S067, P05-S075, P05-S115, P05-S117, P05-S118).
- **A08-AP-75** (P05-AP07; section 7) — The export's clock (A02 K-02's A08 side; P01-S137): the as-of time is a naive literal 15:30 (L158) while the session bounds come from the configured `trading_hours` (L147 to L148); the as-of time ends every window, fixes every cache age at 0 and sets the recency clock; the requested date is checked against neither the trading calendar nor the current date, so a run for today before 15:30 asks for windows that end after the run (P05-S078 to P05-S080, P05-S094).
- **A08-AP-76** (P05-AP08; section 7) — The export's unvalidated options: `--interval` declares no `choices`, and the copy is made with `model_copy`, which does not validate, so the loader's validator ("5minute", "15minute", "30minute") is not applied; a structure interval makes the anchors use the structural list (P02-S042 (c)); `--orb-window` and the dates are not range-checked (P05-S064, P05-S068, P05-S069, P05-S080). Boundary: the validator is A02's.
- **A08-AP-77** (P05-AP09; section 7) — Other texts of the export against its code: "Run on the VM with a live token (like sr_detector_backfill)" against a backfill that uses no broker client or token; "never ... persists" beside the CSV write; "a detector per as-of date" against one detector per symbol and date; the copy's comment about the running system's live object, in a separate process; `MarketWindows` imported and never used (P05-S048, P05-S064, P05-S065, P05-S083, P05-S115).
- **A08-AP-78** (P05-AP10; section 7) — The spot check's decision rule: the verdict turns on a deviation of 0.15 at any consecutive pair in the window, not on the step at the ex-date or on an expected factor; an ordinary move of 15 % or more anywhere gives the UNADJUSTED text, and an unadjusted factor strictly between 0.85 and 1.15 gives the ADJUSTED text when no other pair deviates by 0.15 or more; when no pair is compared the ADJUSTED text is printed; the exit status is 0 for both verdicts; the docstring states no threshold ("near 1.0", "near the split factor") (P05-S030 to P05-S033, P05-S114).
- **A08-AP-79** (P05-AP11; section 7) — The spot check's result has nowhere to go: it is printed only; "note the result in SYSTEM_MAP" is left to the person running it; no document or commit records a run or result; nothing consumes it; the UNADJUSTED text's "detector must adjust or restrict lookback" names a step for which no code exists; "one-time" and "on the VM" are not enforced (A05 HA-07, unchanged) (P05-S017, P05-S034, P05-S036, P05-S039 to P05-S046, P05-S098).
- **A08-AP-80** (P05-AP12; section 7) — The spot check's symbol lookup and candles: its own dump download, keyed on tradingsymbol with the first match kept and no segment or instrument-type check, against the broker's recommended unique key (exchange and tradingsymbol); the docstring's "equity candles" is not checked; the rows are used in the order received (P05-S027 to P05-S029, P05-S099, P05-S103). Boundary: A05 XC-03 and CM-05 are A05's records (unchanged).
- **A08-AP-81** (P05-AP13; section 7) — The spot check's client (interface only, security rules): built from the token file alone, with no timeout argument, no limiter and no handler for a malformed file (P05-S021 to P05-S026, P05-S095 to P05-S097, P05-S104, P05-S105, P05-S113). Boundary: A04 CL-11, CL-12, CL-15, AU-05, RL-06 and A02 K-32 (all unchanged).
- **A08-AP-82** (P05-AP14; section 12) — "spec C" stands for three different things in A08's and A01's texts (the spot check's one-time verification, the closure's pacing, the fetcher's fail-safe), and no such specification is tracked (A05 HA-09 point (4), unchanged) (P05-S108).
- **A08-AP-83** (P05-AP15; sections 9, 11) — The fetcher-count text: the registry's "OhlcFetcher x5" and `main.py`'s five registrations leave out the V1 detector's fetcher, built inside `build_sr_detector` (A05 XC-03's handoff row L3274, A15's text) (P05-S099; P02-S099). Boundary: A15.

### 15.2 What cannot be established (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED), with the evidence that would settle it

Every item below is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (Document 06, section 4.2): both legacy machines are terminated, and no production database, log, journal or deployed configuration survives; a cron declaration, a test or a legacy document is not evidence that anything ran (P07-S109; P06-S006). Each item states, in the source part's words (condensed), what cannot be established and the evidence that would settle it; merged entries keep every source's specifics.

- **A08-U-01** (P01-U01; P02-U02; P03-U02; P04-U04; P06-U01; sections 8, 9) — The deployed values of every configuration key that A08 code reads: the core's parameters (`default_pivot_n`, `pivot_n_by_tf`, `cluster_pct`, `merge_pct`, `band_buffer_pct`, the six weights, `t_high`, `t_med`, `touch_cap`, `volume_bins`, `volume_node_frac`, `recency_window_days`, `recency_min_factor`, and the regime's and V3's pivot `n`) [P01-U01]; the lookbacks, TTLs, re-warm margin, anchor settings and the limiter's `max_wait_sec` [P02-U02]; the V1 observer's switches and parameters, which the arming commit records were changed manually on the machine before being committed [P03-U02]; the V2 keys (`wait_for_retest_enabled`, `near_zone_buffer_pct`, `require_confidence`, `retest_timeout_sec`, `retest_max_away_pct`, `confirm_strong_close_frac`, `breakout_margin_pct`, `onem_lookback_days`, `retest_poll_interval_sec`, `sl_buffer_pct`) [P04-U04]; and every key of the block and `structure_exit_enabled`, so which switch row of section 9 the process was in on any day, and which files and values were changed directly on a legacy machine and when (A07 XS-04, RS-23, unchanged) [P06-U01] (P01-S048, P01-S055, P01-S065, P01-S069; P02-S040, P02-S045; P03-S019, P03-S025, P03-S214; P04-S008, P04-S128; P06-S007, P06-S185). Would settle it: the configuration files as deployed on the production machine, the effective configuration logged at boot, or the production `config_snapshots` rows (A02's snapshotter), none of which survives; the legacy documents' statements are claims, and the loader has no overlay the repository would show.
- **A08-U-02** (P01-U02; P02-U03; P03-U03; P04-U05; sections 3, 4, 5, 6, 10) — The legacy server's candles: whether the historical rows reached `from_kite` in ascending order, without duplicates or gaps, and whether the last row was a still-forming bar [P01-U02]; whether the answers included a still-forming last candle (intraday, or today's daily candle during the session), and so which day `prior_day_levels` took and what the anchors measured [P02-U03]; how long after each placement the V1 analysis ran [P03-U03]; and, for the minute candles, whether a label marked the start or the end of its minute and whether rows were always ascending and unique [P04-U05] (P01-S021, P01-S046, P01-S048, P01-S094 to P01-S096; P02-S024, P02-S026, P02-S048, P02-S058; P03-S053, P03-S057, P03-S168; P04-S028, P04-S066 to P04-S068; A05 CM-05, HA-03, HA-04). Would settle it: captured broker answers of the legacy period, and the production rows' `ts` and `created_at`.
- **A08-U-03** (P01-U03; section 3) — Whether any `None`, non-numeric, NaN, infinite, zero or negative price, or a missing or `null` volume, ever reached `from_kite` or the algorithms, and so whether any exception or silent outcome of the core occurred (P01-S017, P01-S018, P01-S097; A05 CM-10, HA-08). ## 16. Draft questions about intent for Rama

Only where the code and the legacy sources cannot answer. These are drafts gathered from the parts and merged where they overlap: none has been raised with Rama, each stays OPEN FOR RAMA, and nothing is decided in this STUDY (Document 04, section 4, step 8). Each asks only how the legacy system was meant to work, or whether a legacy record exists, never about the new system; the options are not ranked and no answer is preselected. The rules that the docstrings state (SR-P1, SR-P2, SR-Z1 to SR-Z4, SR-C1 to SR-C3) are not asked again (P01 section 12), and the history questions of the 16 Open Issues Register entries are not put again: they stay OPEN as recorded (P07-S068, P07-S114). P07 has no question of its own (P07-S114). For each: the question with its options, the source IDs, the points and unknown items it bears on, and the STUDY section.

**The calculation core (section 3).**

- **A08-Q-01** (P01-R1; A08-AP-01) — In the legacy code, a support or resistance zone was rated HIGH confidence whenever swing highs or lows at about the same price appeared on two of the three timeframes (with at least three swing points in total), or on all three, even if no other evidence (a high-volume price, yesterday's high, low or close, or a round number) was there. Was that what you meant by HIGH confidence? A: yes, agreement of swings across timeframes was enough; B: no, HIGH was meant to need at least one kind of evidence other than the swings; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-02** (P01-R2; A08-AP-03, A08-AP-04) — The legacy code let one zone grow as wide as the chain of nearby swing points allowed (there was no maximum width), and it treated a single swing point as a zone of its own. Did you intend a limit on how wide a zone could be, or a minimum number of swing points for a zone? A: no limit and no minimum were intended; B: a width limit was intended; C: a minimum number of touches was intended; D: both; E: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-03** (P01-R3; A08-AP-02) — When two or more neighbouring candles had exactly the same high (or low) at a turning point, the legacy code counted each of them as a separate touch of the zone. Did you mean such a flat top or bottom to count as one touch or as several? A: one touch; B: several touches; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-04** (P01-R4; A08-AP-14, A08-U-08) — The code comments say the swing-based zones were "NOT-YET-VALIDATED" until your manual chart-marking check, and that the weights and thresholds were to be tuned in a "visual-review phase". Did you carry out the manual-marking check or the visual review, and is a record of the result kept anywhere? A: neither was done; B: one or both were done (please say which, when, and where the result is); C: not sure; Other. (A08-Q-14 asks the related question about the calibration decision.)

**Candle fetch, zone cache and warmer (section 4).**

- **A08-Q-05** (P02-R1; A08-AP-23, A08-AP-22) — In the legacy code, the zone warmer (built only when the retest or the structure-exit switch is on; both are off in the committed file) keeps refreshing a stock's support and resistance zones, once it has worked on that stock, about every 25 minutes (at the file's settings) for as long as the program runs, after the market closes as well, and it never drops a stock from its list; a stock whose refresh gets no price data at all is retried about every 2 seconds. Was the refreshing meant to stop at some point? A: no, refreshing for as long as the program ran was intended; B: it was meant to stop at the market close; C: it was meant to stop once the stock had no live signal or trade; D: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-06** (P02-R2; A08-AP-26) — To find the previous day's high, low and close, the legacy code takes the second-to-last daily candle it receives, assuming the last one is today's. If the broker sent no candle for today (for example before the market opened), the code would take the day before that. Which day did you intend? A: always the last fully completed trading day; B: the second-to-last candle, as coded; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-07** (P02-R3; A08-AP-24) — In the legacy code (again only when the retest or the structure-exit switch is on), the zones that the retest check and the structure exit read are kept for up to 30 minutes, but the price data under them has its own 30-minute store, so a zone can rest on price data up to about an hour old while it appears at most 30 minutes old. Did you intend a limit on how old the price data behind a zone could be? A: yes, about 30 minutes for the price data itself; B: no particular limit; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-08** (P02-R4; A08-AP-18) — The legacy price-data store keeps one list per stock and time-frame, whatever length of history is asked for, so a request for one day of 30-minute bars and a request for six months of 30-minute bars can be answered with the same list (at the committed settings no part of the program asks for both). Did you intend those two requests to be kept apart? A: yes, they were meant to be kept apart; B: no, sharing one list was intended; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-09** (P02-R5; A08-AP-25) — When part of a stock's price data fails to arrive (for example the daily bars fail but the hourly bars come), the legacy code still builds and stores zones from what has arrived, and then takes the "previous day" levels from the hourly bars. Did you intend zones to be built from incomplete data? A: yes, zones from part of the data were acceptable; B: no, all time-frames were required; C: no, but zones without the daily bars alone were acceptable; D: not sure; Other.

**The V1 observer and the backfill (section 5).**

- **A08-Q-10** (P03-R1; A08-AP-36, A08-U-02) — When the S&R observer studied a placed trade, it fetched the chart data at the moment it got round to that trade, which could be minutes after the entry, and it did not cut the candles off at the entry time. Did you mean the observer's verdict to use only the candles up to the moment of entry? A: yes, only up to the entry; B: no, the latest candles were fine; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-11** (P03-R2; A08-AP-40, A08-AP-41) — The 15:58 backfill filled in the result only for trades already closed; a trade still open at that time (a delivery trade, for example) was left blank, and no later scheduled run went back to it. A manual run for that date was also skipped when it was started on a weekend or holiday. How were such trades meant to get their result? A: a later scheduled run was meant to pick them up; B: by a manual re-run when needed; C: they did not need a result; D: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-12** (P03-R3; A08-AP-37) — The "weak breakout" and "no volume confirmation" flags compared the last 30-minute candle with the nearest zone below the entry (above it for a short), even when that zone was far away and the last candle had not crossed it. Did you mean these flags to apply only when a recent candle had actually broken through a nearby zone? A: yes, only a real recent break of a nearby zone; B: no, the rule as coded was intended; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-13** (P03-R4; A08-AP-39, A08-U-17) — A placed trade could end with no row in the S&R table (a full queue, an error during the analysis, a failed write, a shutdown or crash, or an order whose placement call timed out although the order may have gone through), leaving only a log line or nothing. Was the table meant to hold a row for every placed trade? A: yes, every placed trade; B: missing rows were acceptable for a shadow log; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-14** (P03-R5; A08-U-23) — The legacy notes say the observer's rows and their end-of-day results were being collected to decide whether to move on to the "wait for retest" feature or to recalibrate the zones, and that this decision was still open at the end of July 2026. Was that decision ever made, and is a record of it kept anywhere? A: it was not made; B: it was made (please say what was decided, when, and where it is recorded); C: not sure; Other. (A08-Q-04 asks the related question about the manual chart-marking.)

**The V2 retest path (section 6).**

- **A08-Q-15** (P04-R1; P06-R5; A08-AP-51, A08-U-01, A08-U-05) — The "wait for retest" entry (when a signal's price was inside a strong resistance zone for a buy, or a strong support zone for a sell, the signal was held back, and a market order was placed only after a breakout, a retest of the zone and a strong one-minute close) is switched off in the committed settings, as are the structure-based exit and the VWAP/opening-range anchors. Do you recall switching on any of them on the production machine at any time, even for a test (for example by editing the settings file there)? A: never; B: yes (please say which and roughly when); C: not sure; Other. Note: P06-R5 is narrower than A07-Q19, which Rama answered on 24-Nov-2026 (RS-23: tracked configuration files were changed both locally and directly on the VM), and than Open Issues Register entry A07-29, answered OPEN on 25-Nov-2026; Claude kept it, with this overlap noted, for Rama to see in context (Claude's assignment decisions, section 13.6).
- **A08-Q-16** (P04-R2; A08-AP-52) — When the end-of-day square-off cleared a candidate that was still waiting for its retest, its signal kept the status "waiting" and was never given a final status. Was such a signal meant to get a final status at the end of the day? A: yes, a final status (for example "expired"); B: no, "waiting" was acceptable; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-17** (P04-R3; A08-AP-53) — While one candidate of a share was waiting for its retest, a new signal for the same share was refused only if its price was also inside a strong zone; otherwise it was placed in the normal way, and once the first candidate had been confirmed a new one could be held back again. Which rule did you mean? A: only one retest-path trade per share at a time; B: refuse only the signals that would themselves wait for a retest; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-18** (P04-R4; A08-AP-55) — For the confirming one-minute candle, the setting "strong close 0.6" was coded as: for a buy, the close must be at least 60 % of the way up the candle's range (its top 40 %); for a sell, the mirror. Some comments describe it as "in the strong (upper) 60 %" of the candle, which would also accept a close 40 % of the way up. Which did you mean? A: at least 60 % of the way up, as coded; B: anywhere in the upper 60 %; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-19** (P04-R5; A08-AP-59) — After a restart, the retest monitor restored every waiting candidate in its table, including any left from an earlier day. Were candidates from an earlier day meant to be restored? A: no, only the same day's; B: yes, all of them; C: not sure; Other.

**The scripts (section 7).**

- **A08-Q-20** (P05-R1; A08-AP-78) — The one-time check of whether the broker's past prices are corrected for share splits and bonus issues decided "corrected" or "not corrected" by looking for any day-to-day jump of 15 % or more in the closing price anywhere in the date range around the event, not only on the event date itself. Was the check meant to judge only the price change at the event date, or any day in the range? A: only the event date; B: any day in the range was intended; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-21** (P05-R2; A08-AP-74) — The level-export script, meant for comparing the system's support and resistance levels with your own chart marks, writes nothing for a stock and date whose price data could not be fetched, so its file cannot show that data were missing. Was the export meant to show such gaps in its file? A: yes, gaps were to be shown; B: no, leaving them out was intended; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-22** (P05-R3; A08-AP-75) — The level-export script computes each date's levels as they stood at 15:30 on that date. Was it meant only for trading days that had already closed, or also for the current day while the market was open? A: closed days only; B: also the current day during market hours; C: not sure; Other.

**Configuration and wiring (sections 8, 9).**

- **A08-Q-23** (P06-R2; A08-AP-44) — The legacy system had three separate settings for how far beyond a support or resistance level the stop-loss is placed: one for the V1 observer's suggested stop (0.3 %), one for the "wait for retest" entry (0.2 %) and one for the structure-based exit (0.2 %, with a note saying it should match the entry setting). Were the entry and the exit meant to use one shared value? A: yes, one shared value; B: no, separate values on purpose; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-24** (P06-R3; A08-AP-31) — The support and resistance tuning settings were also used by the newer V3 decision chain and the PB-01 watchlist, and one cache setting by the market-regime engine, so changing an S&R setting changed those features too. Was that sharing intended? A: yes, one set of settings for all; B: no, each feature was meant to have its own; C: not sure; Other.
- **A08-Q-25** (P06-R4; A08-AP-34) — The code allowed the structure-based exit to be switched on without the "wait for retest" feature. In that case its support and resistance zones were prepared only for stocks that had received a new signal since the program started. Was the structure-based exit meant to be used on its own? A: yes, on its own; B: no, only together with the retest feature; C: it was never meant to be switched on yet; D: not sure; Other.

**Withdrawn (not to be put).** P06-R1 (A08-AP-63; A01 C13) was withdrawn by the P06 checker: it asked whether the S&R background workers were meant to start only after the start-up reconcile and the kill-switch check, which is the history question of Open Issues Register entry A01-10 (C13), whose status reads "History: OPEN QUESTION (no historical answer; recorded OPEN at Rama's direction; confirmed on 05-Nov-2026). Treatment: AGREED (06-Nov-2026)."; A08 records code facts only and does not put the question again. Claude accepted the withdrawal (section 13.6).

FACT (counts): 27 question IDs from the parts (P01-R1 to R4; P02-R1 to R5; P03-R1 to R5; P04-R1 to R5; P05-R1 to R3; P06-R1 to R5) are carried as 25 draft questions, A08-Q-01 to A08-Q-25 (P04-R1 and P06-R5 merged as A08-Q-15), plus the withdrawn P06-R1; P07 has none.
